        "title": "Public transport in Morocco – busses, taxis and more",
        "description": "## Petit Taxi in Moroccan cities\nSo let’s start small. After arriving at the airport you are probably looking for transport to your hotel or riad. You can of course book a transfer through your accommodation, but it will most likely be cheaper to just get a petit taxi.\n\nDepending on the city you’re in, they have different colours. Red in [Fez](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/unique-things-to-do-fes/), blue in [Rabat](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/things-to-do-rabat/), you get the picture. For transfers from airport or train station to the [medina](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/medina-and-souk/) they normally have a fixed price which is most likely visible somewhere either in the car or at the taxi station. In Marrakech there is now a taxi stand outside that sells tickets for fixed prices. So no more getting ripped off.\n\nIf you just want to get from point A to B you can find petit taxis on almost any road and you can always wave them and tell your destination. Prices depend on the number of passengers and you should discuss before getting in. Depending on the distance the price when you’re sharing should be between 5 and 15 MAD, do not believe them when they want to change you much more than this! In a few cities taxies actually use the meter, e.g. in Meknes and Tangier I never had any discussions about this.\n\n![Pink taxis in Kelaat M'Gouna Morocco. getting around Morocco](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/pink-taxis.jpg)Pink taxis in the city of roses, Kelaat M’Gouna\n\n## Grand taxis between Moroccan cities\nFor anything outside the city you’re in, you will need to look at the grand taxis. It can actually be quite the experience to take a grand taxi in Morocco, so you should use it at least once. There are fixed stations for these in the cities and towns. Sometimes more than one! Make sure to ask your accommodation for the right station for your destination.\n\n![grand taxis Morocco, public transport in Morocco](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/taxi-station.jpg)Grand taxi station in Rissani\n\nOnce you get there, find the man in charge. He will tell you which car is leaving to your destination next and how much it costs. Before the taxi leaves (which it does when it’s full), he will collect the money. Short rides to close towns cost around 15 MAD, longer trips of around 100 km around 50 MAD. The longer the ride, the higher the price.\n\nIf you are in a hurry and don’t want to wait, you can always buy additional seats. But beware, they stuff the cars and buying all seats adds up! There are still some very old Mercedes around where they expect 2 people to share the passenger seat and 4 people in the back. The government is trying to shut these down and is handing out credits to drivers to buy new model Dacia’s or similar which seat 6 people.\n\n![grand taxi morocco, grey mercedes](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/Mercedes.jpg)Probably the crappiest taxi with the craziest driver ever!\n\nThe longest I ever had to wait for a grand taxi to arrive and fill up was around 75 minutes, so you always need to calculate a little extra time.\n\nDon’t be surprised if the car stops on the way in or out of the towns. They often collect and drop off items someone is sending to their relatives or friends.\n\n## Taxis in Marrakech\n[Marrakech](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/12-best-things-to-do-in-marrakech/) is an exception, as it has both small and big taxis within the city. Big taxis are shared with a fixed price of 6 DH (June 21) and if you are going into the suburbs you will have to change taxis at certain fixed stations.\n\nSmall taxis can be shared too at a reduced price, but normally should start the meter. Ask for the counter to be turned on and really insist, otherwise they will rip you off. Last week I wanted to go a short distance to the train station. One empty shared taxi stopped and told me 100 DH to go there. I sent him away with a curse and waited for a small taxi, which cost me 16 DH for the ride.\n\nFrom the airport you can now get a ticket for a fixed price, the stand is just outside the airport exit.\n\n## Buses in Morocco\nI guess the most commonly used public transportation in Morocco is the bus. There are 2 good bus companies catering to tourists, [CTM](https://ctm.ma/) and [Supratours](https://supratours.ma/). You can get the tickets online or at the stations, again beware, they might not be at the regular bus station (gare rotiere) and most of the times getting the ticket a day before travel is early enough.\n\nRegarding the prices for Supratours buses in Morocco, a ticket from [Marrakech](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/12-best-things-to-do-in-marrakech/) to [Merzouga](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/complete-guide-merzouga/) currently costs 240 MAD.\n\nIf you have luggage to check in, you have to be at the bus station 30 minutes before departure to get your luggage checked. Sometimes they weigh it, sometimes the price is per bag and is around 5-10 MAD.\n\nThere are other buses in Morocco too, lot’s of them actually, but they are mostly used by locals. They have less space per passenger, go shorter distances, but might be an alternative if the CTM bus and Supratours don’t go to your destination.\n\n![supratours bus in morocco, public transport in morocco](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/Supratours.jpg)The Supratours bus\n\nI crossed half of Morocco on Supratours buses and what I remember most, is that in the mountains the local women all vomit! The smell was so bad and also the liquid ran all over the floor of the bus. So I suggest you only take small luggage on board to make sure it fits into the overhead space.\n\n## Trains in Morocco\nTrain travel in Morocco is very easy. But the train system in Morocco covers only the northern part of the country, north of the Atlas Mountains. So you can take the train from [Marrakech](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/ultimate-marrakech-travel-guide/) to Casablanca or [Fez](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/ultimate-fes-travel-guide/), but not to Agadir or Zagora for example. I found the trains to be fairly reliable in terms of punctuality. Trains between the bigger cities go every 2 hours, so there are plenty of options.\n\nYou can choose between 1st and 2nd class seats, the main difference is less people, especially less locals in first class. For me, travelling like and with the locals is half the fun of train travel, but pricewise you can easily travel first class, as it is very affordable. A train ticket from Casablanca to [Marrakech](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/12-best-things-to-do-in-marrakech/) for example costs 49 MAD on a non-flexible second class ticket, or 150 MAD in first class.\n\nYou can check for connections and prices at the [ONCF website](https://www.oncf.ma/en/) but I had to buy my tickets at the station as the website was down a lot when I was travelling in Morocco by train.\n\n## Planes within Morocco\nThere are lots of in-country flights available with Royal Air Moroc and they are very affordable. A ticket from Casablanca to Errachidia for example costs around 400 MAD and only takes just over one hour. If you compare this to taking the train from Casablanca to Marrakech (2,5 hours) and then the bus from Marrakech to Errachidia (10 hours) with a total price of around 240 MAD it is actually worth considering. From Errachidia it is only 2 hours with the bus to [Merzouga, if you fancy a desert adventure](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/complete-guide-merzouga/).\n\nRyanair now also offers domestic flights as does Air Arabia.\n\n![](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/06/RAM-plane.jpg)Typical domestic plane in Morocco\n\n## Car rentals\n#### 8 Comments\n- ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/f1b136f8bc6d3a6be488a70ca6a83291?s=120&d=identicon&r=g) [Eva Westerling](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/)Post Author[4 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-968)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-968) Hi Foz, To be honest, with that little time, I would not drive all that way. I would spend the time in Marrakech and maybe go to Essaouira for a day or two. But if you really want to, you will have to rent a car or organise a driver. There is only one bus per day, and that leaves before you arrive and on the way back will get into Marrakech after your flight. Please be aware, the drive with a couple of stops takes 12 hours or more. And it is already very hot here in Merzouga.\n\n### Write A Comment[Cancel Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#respond)\n- [Home](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/)\n- [Destinations](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/category/destinations/)\n  -         "markdown": "Are you planning a holiday to Morocco and are unsure of how to get around? Let me help you out! During my almost 6 years here, I’ve used every available kind of public transport in Morocco there is. From planes to trains to taxis, I will tell you everything there is to know.\n\nIt can be quite adventurous to travel like the locals and not everything is advisable if you are short on time. But I promise, you will see Morocco from a different perspective! It is, of course, also a [great way to save money](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/save-money-morocco/).\n\nI will talk about:\n\n[Petit taxis](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#petit-taxi)\n\n[Grand taxis](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#grand-taxi)\n\n[Busses](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#buses-morocco)\n\n[Trains](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#trains-morocco)\n\n[Planes](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#planes-morocco)\n\n[Car rentals](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#carrental-morocco)\n\n## Petit Taxi in Moroccan cities\n\nSo let’s start small. After arriving at the airport you are probably looking for transport to your hotel or riad. You can of course book a transfer through your accommodation, but it will most likely be cheaper to just get a petit taxi.\n\nDepending on the city you’re in, they have different colours. Red in [Fez](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/unique-things-to-do-fes/), blue in [Rabat](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/things-to-do-rabat/), you get the picture. For transfers from airport or train station to the [medina](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/medina-and-souk/) they normally have a fixed price which is most likely visible somewhere either in the car or at the taxi station. In Marrakech there is now a taxi stand outside that sells tickets for fixed prices. So no more getting ripped off.\n\nIf you just want to get from point A to B you can find petit taxis on almost any road and you can always wave them and tell your destination. Prices depend on the number of passengers and you should discuss before getting in. Depending on the distance the price when you’re sharing should be between 5 and 15 MAD, do not believe them when they want to change you much more than this! In a few cities taxies actually use the meter, e.g. in Meknes and Tangier I never had any discussions about this.\n\n![Pink taxis in Kelaat M'Gouna Morocco. getting around Morocco](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/pink-taxis.jpg)Pink taxis in the city of roses, Kelaat M’Gouna\n\n#### _Funny story:_\n\nWhen I got out of the Rabat train station and was looking for a taxi for an approximately 800 m ride (very heavy luggage), every single taxi driver told me it’s 35 MAD. But I knew it should be less than 10. So I asked a local lady walking in front of me, if she could help me. She tried a few taxis with the same result. Then she told me to follow her to the other side of the road, where she just waved a taxi, told the driver where I wanted to go and got into the taxi with me. When I got out, she left with me and I paid 6 MAD for the both of us.\n\nBut in general, I advise you to just walk around in the\ncities! There is so much to discover that you won’t see out of a taxi. And in\nthe medinas you have to walk anyways as the alleys are much to small for cars.\n\nIf you are worried about getting lost or scammed by the locals, download the [maps.me app](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/best-travel-apps/), it’s the best offline map available and it shows much more detail than google maps in the medinas of Morocco!\n\n## Grand taxis between Moroccan cities\n\nFor anything outside the city you’re in, you will need to\nlook at the grand taxis. It can actually be quite the experience to take a\ngrand taxi in Morocco, so you should use it at least once. There are fixed\nstations for these in the cities and towns. Sometimes more than one! Make sure\nto ask your accommodation for the right station for your destination.\n\n![grand taxis Morocco, public transport in Morocco](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/taxi-station.jpg)Grand taxi station in Rissani\n\nOnce you get there, find the man in charge. He will tell\nyou which car is leaving to your destination next and how much it costs. Before\nthe taxi leaves (which it does when it’s full), he will collect the money.\nShort rides to close towns cost around 15 MAD, longer trips of around 100 km\naround 50 MAD. The longer the ride, the higher the price.\n\nIf you are in a hurry and don’t want to wait, you can\nalways buy additional seats. But beware, they stuff the cars and buying all\nseats adds up! There are still some very old Mercedes around where they expect\n2 people to share the passenger seat and 4 people in the back. The government\nis trying to shut these down and is handing out credits to drivers to buy new\nmodel Dacia’s or similar which seat 6 people.\n\n![grand taxi morocco, grey mercedes](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/Mercedes.jpg)Probably the crappiest taxi with the craziest driver ever!\n\nThe longest I ever had to wait for a grand taxi to arrive\nand fill up was around 75 minutes, so you always need to calculate a little\nextra time.\n\nDon’t be surprised if the car stops on the way in or out\nof the towns. They often collect and drop off items someone is sending to their\nrelatives or friends.\n\n## Taxis in Marrakech\n\n[Marrakech](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/12-best-things-to-do-in-marrakech/) is an exception, as it has both small and big taxis within the city. Big taxis are shared with a fixed price of 6 DH (June 21) and if you are going into the suburbs you will have to change taxis at certain fixed stations.\n\nSmall taxis can be shared too at a reduced price, but normally should start the meter. Ask for the counter to be turned on and really insist, otherwise they will rip you off. Last week I wanted to go a short distance to the train station. One empty shared taxi stopped and told me 100 DH to go there. I sent him away with a curse and waited for a small taxi, which cost me 16 DH for the ride.\n\nFrom the airport you can now get a ticket for a fixed price, the stand is just outside the airport exit.\n\n## Buses in Morocco\n\nI guess the most commonly used public transportation in Morocco is the bus. There are 2 good bus companies catering to tourists, [CTM](https://ctm.ma/) and [Supratours](https://supratours.ma/). You can get the tickets online or at the stations, again beware, they might not be at the regular bus station (gare rotiere) and most of the times getting the ticket a day before travel is early enough.\n\nRegarding the prices for Supratours buses in Morocco, a ticket from [Marrakech](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/12-best-things-to-do-in-marrakech/) to [Merzouga](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/complete-guide-merzouga/) currently costs 240 MAD.\n\nIf you have luggage to check in, you have to be at the bus\nstation 30 minutes before departure to get your luggage checked. Sometimes they\nweigh it, sometimes the price is per bag and is around 5-10 MAD.\n\nThere are other buses in Morocco too, lot’s of them actually, but they are mostly used by locals. They have less space per passenger, go shorter distances, but might be an alternative if the CTM bus and Supratours don’t go to your destination.\n\n![supratours bus in morocco, public transport in morocco](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/Supratours.jpg)The Supratours bus\n\nI crossed half of Morocco on Supratours buses and what I remember most, is that in the mountains the local women all vomit! The smell was so bad and also the liquid ran all over the floor of the bus. So I suggest you only take small luggage on board to make sure it fits into the overhead space.\n\n## Trains in Morocco\n\nTrain travel in Morocco is very easy. But the train system in Morocco covers only the northern part of the country, north of the Atlas Mountains. So you can take the train from [Marrakech](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/ultimate-marrakech-travel-guide/) to Casablanca or [Fez](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/ultimate-fes-travel-guide/), but not to Agadir or Zagora for example. I found the trains to be fairly reliable in terms of punctuality. Trains between the bigger cities go every 2 hours, so there are plenty of options.\n\nYou can choose between 1st and 2nd class seats, the main difference is less people, especially less locals in first class. For me, travelling like and with the locals is half the fun of train travel, but pricewise you can easily travel first class, as it is very affordable.  A train ticket from Casablanca to [Marrakech](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/12-best-things-to-do-in-marrakech/) for example costs 49 MAD on a non-flexible second class ticket, or 150 MAD in first class.\n\nYou can check for connections and prices at the [ONCF website](https://www.oncf.ma/en/) but I had to buy my tickets at the station as the website was down a lot when I was travelling in Morocco by train.\n\n## Planes within Morocco\n\nThere are lots of in-country flights available with Royal Air Moroc and they are very affordable. A ticket from Casablanca to Errachidia for example costs around 400 MAD and only takes just over one hour. If you compare this to taking the train from Casablanca to Marrakech (2,5 hours) and then the bus from Marrakech to Errachidia (10 hours) with a total price of around 240 MAD it is actually worth considering. From Errachidia it is only 2 hours with the bus to [Merzouga, if you fancy a desert adventure](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/complete-guide-merzouga/).\n\nRyanair now also offers domestic flights as does Air Arabia.\n\n![](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/06/RAM-plane.jpg)Typical domestic plane in Morocco\n\n### Is my blog helping you?\n\nMaybe you want to thank me by inviting me for a coffee!\n\n[![Ko-fi donations](https://storage.ko-fi.com/cdn/cup-border.png)Buy me a coffee!](https://ko-fi.com/P5P4JE5TW \"Support me on ko-fi.com\")\n\n## Car rentals\n\nEven though renting a car in Morocco is not considered\npublic transport, I will include it here, because it is a great option to get\naround and have more flexibility.\n\nIt might be relatively hard to find good car rental options online before you get here. They are mostly limited to the airports in the bigger cities. But, you can easily find good car rentals once you are here, even in smaller cities. Just ask your accommodation and they will point you in the right direction.\n\n![rental car, grey, renault clio, car rental in morocco](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/04/car2.jpg)My little rental car on my first road trip in Morocco\n\nPrices for rental cars start from 250 MAD per day for a\nsmall car using benzine and 330 MAD for small diesels. For bigger cars and\nespecially 4x4s the prices are much higher and vary from company to company, so\nit is good to shop around a bit. The longer the period you want to rent the car\nfor, the more likely you can discuss the price and get a discount.\n\nIf you are looking for a recommendation for a rental company let me know in the comments or shoot me an [email](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/contact-me/). They are based in Errachidia but will deliver the car to your location for a small extra fee.\n\nSo, I hope this guide on how to travel around Morocco is\nhelpful for planning your holiday in Morocco. Let me know about your experiences\nin the comments!\n\n_**For your trip planning, also look at these posts:**_\n\n[![](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/Marrakech-Sightseeing.jpg)](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/12-best-things-to-do-in-marrakech/)\n\n[![](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/Fes-Sightseeing.jpg)](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/unique-things-to-do-fes/)\n\n[![](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/UNESCO-sites.jpg)](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/unesco-sites-morocco/)\n\n![](https://bucket.mlcdn.com/a/3000/3000646/images/113b58b099146398086180673359d7df0b709190.jpeg)\n\n#### Monthly Newsletter\n\nNever miss any new posts or life updates. That income goes to supporting this website and keeping it free for you and everyone else!_\n\n![](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/Pin-Transport-6.jpg)\n\n![](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/10/Pin-Transport-1.jpg)\n\n#### 8 Comments\n\n1. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/1a01221e0ed1ec7aedf30043d3e01da3?s=120&d=identicon&r=g)\n\n\n\n\n\nMaya[3 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-1065)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-1065)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nHi Eva!\n\n\nGreat Blog, thank you. Will be “buying you coffee”.\n\n\nAny advise on a single female traveling by rental car from Tangier towards the Eastern Mediterranean Coast? Safe?\n\n\n\nThank you!\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/f1b136f8bc6d3a6be488a70ca6a83291?s=120&d=identicon&r=g)\n\n\n\n\n\n     [Eva Westerling](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/)Post Author[3 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-1070)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-1070)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n     Hi Maya,\n\n\n     Thank you!\n\n\n     I would not think twice about renting a car and driving around in the north of Morocco, it’s as safe as everything else. Traffic in the cities is sometimes a bit crazy, you just need to be patient. And keep to the speed limit, there are lots of radar controls.\n2. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/129e590d06547492a53058ad4c554916?s=120&d=identicon&r=g)\n\n\n\n\n\nFoz[4 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-967)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-967)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nHi Eva,\n\n\n\nHelpful post by the way! Me and my wife are planning to visit Morocco this April. We only have 4 days; we will arrive at Marrakesh on 9am and flight back on the 4th day from Marrakesh at 1930pm.\n\n\n\nWe are eager to visit Marzouga desert. With the short time frame at hand, we are thinking to rent a car from Marrakesh. Is it something that you think will be feasible? Or do you think is it still alright to go there by bus?\n\n\n\nCheers,\n\n\nFoz\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/f1b136f8bc6d3a6be488a70ca6a83291?s=120&d=identicon&r=g)\n\n\n\n\n\n     [Eva Westerling](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/)Post Author[4 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-968)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-968)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n     Hi Foz,\n\n\n     To be honest, with that little time, I would not drive all that way. I would spend the time in Marrakech and maybe go to Essaouira for a day or two. But if you really want to, you will have to rent a car or organise a driver. There is only one bus per day, and that leaves before you arrive and on the way back will get into Marrakech after your flight. Please be aware, the drive with a couple of stops takes 12 hours or more. And it is already very hot here in Merzouga.\n3. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/eea657b89c51f190270378d9f5d96bc0?s=120&d=identicon&r=g)\n\n\n\n\n\nJudy[4 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-930)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-930)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nHi there\n\n\nWe may be attending a wedding in Marrakesh in sept.\n\n\nWe come from Cape Town (South Africa) and thought of travelling to Portugal and Spain first and the perhaps flying I cross or by ferry to Tangier then hiring a car and driving down vis chefchaouen\n\n\n\nAny perhaps flying back to Spain.\n\n\nIt’s just my hubby and I. We are just in our 60’s but fit and well.\n\n\nAny thoughts?\n\n\n\nThx for your informative blog\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/f1b136f8bc6d3a6be488a70ca6a83291?s=120&d=identicon&r=g)\n\n\n\n\n\n     [Eva Westerling](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/)Post Author[4 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-931)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-931)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n     Hi Judy,\n\n\n     Sounds like a great plan! Just make sure it is possible to drop the rental in a different city. Most car rental companies here are small private ones, not big chains with offices in many cities. So it might be harder to find one that accommodates this. It is really easy to do this route with public transport also. There are buses and taxis to Chefchaouen and from there to Fes. And then you can easily take trains.\n\n\n     Feel free to message me with more questions!\n4. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/9bf76439458e9d09e131e8e7909f8171?s=120&d=identicon&r=g)\n\n\n\n\n\nColin[5 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-248)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-248)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nHi ime planning on travelling from the uk to gambia in october,through morocco, with my small dog, using public transport, will it be allowed on, trains, buses and the big taxis, kind regards colin roper\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n   - ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/f1b136f8bc6d3a6be488a70ca6a83291?s=120&d=identicon&r=g)\n\n\n\n\n\n     [Eva Westerling](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/)Post Author[5 years ago](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-249)[Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/#comment-249)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n     Hi Colin, I don’t think dogs are allowed on public transport in Morocco. At least I’ve never seen any and most Moroccans don’t like dogs. Maybe on trains, but definitely not in taxis or buses. Also, I would not count on the land borders to Mauretania being open by October.\n\n### Write A Comment[Cancel Reply](https://notscaredofthejetlag.com/getting-around-morocco-public-transport/\\#respond)\n\nΔ\n\nThis site uses Akismet to reduce spam.         "url": "https://www.alksar.com/en/get-around-marrakech-bus-taxi-airport-transfer-train-station/",
        "title": "Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station",
        "description": "# How to Get Around Marrakech Bus Taxi Airport Transfer Train Station\n… Find below all the information you need to know about how to **GET AROUND MARRAKECH** bus taxi airport transfer train station, easily and cheaply. To avoid unpleasant surprises, it is strongly recommended that you know the following useful tips before venturing into the streets of the red city.\n\n**In the city, walk, use taxi, take a ride in a horse-drawn carriage, bus or bicycle** without worries.\n\nBelow you will also find the best easy, reliable and economical **transport options to REACH THE OTHER CITIES of the Kingdom, such as Casablanca, [Essaouira](https://www.alksar.com/en/marrakech-morocco-travel-tourism/excursions/essaouira-morocco/) or Tangier or the international airport Marrakech Menara, by train, bus or car.**\n\n![](https://www.alksar.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/RAK-se-deplacer-dans-marrakech-en-caleche-300x150.jpg)\n\n## 3/By Petit Taxis in Marrakech up to 3 persons\n#### Green Taxis\nThere is a company of small taxis: **Green Taxis** 0524 40 94 99. This company charges a surcharge of 10dhs per day and 15dhs per night at the public rate. Please have the meter connected. Beyond the city centre, a flat rate of 100dhs per day is applied.\n\nGreen taxis can come to pick you up at a precise address, with some discrepancies of punctuality from time to time.\n\nJust so you know, little taxis have a number on their doors. It is necessary to note it in case of big problems, in addition to the day and time, but in general everything goes very well once the question of the fare is raised.Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAwCgu/QABnoqoRgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\nRiad Al Ksar Hotel of Charm Swimming pool Marrakech medina\n\n**[VISIT RIAD AL KSAR & SPA, Near Jemaa El Fnaa Square & Bahia Palace, in the Medina](https://www.alksar.com/en/hotel-rooms/luxury-hotel/)**\n\n## 6/ Bus in the centre of Marrakech\nThe Marrakech Bus network is operated by ALSA [[http://www.alsa.ma/fr/marrakech](http://www.alsa.ma/fr/marrakech)](http://www.alsa.ma/fr/marrakech), present throughout Morocco. The fare of the **bus ticket is 4 dhs and can be bought on board**. There is an IKLASS card to be recharged and which allows you to pay 3.50dhs per ticket. Buses are comfortable and sometimes air-conditioned. It is one more way to get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station.\n\nIt is possible to travel by bus, from the **bus station** to the Medina (Arset El Bilk, next to Jemaa el Fna) with **buses number 8 or 10**.\n\n**Bus number 1, very practical for tourists**, connects the Medina and the new city.The stops are: Djemaa el Fna Square (Arset El Bilk) in front of the square Foucault (between the place Djemaa el Fna and the mosque of the Moutoubia), **Gueliz la Grande Poste** (Place du 16 novembre), GuelizCarre Eden and it passes near the Majorelle garden and the Bahia/Badi palaces.\n\n![reseau bus marrakech](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAACAAAAAccCAYAAAAw+2E5AAAACXBIWXMAAA7EAAAOxAGVKw4bAAAgAElEQVR42uzBAQEAAACAkP6v7ggKAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA bus marrakech\n\nSince the COP 22 in 2016, the city of Marrakech has been equipped **with”high service level” (BHNS) electric buses**. **4 Lines are planned with frequencies between 5 and 10 minutes in specific lanes.**\n\n**Buses starting with L (L22…)** are the suburban buses that serve the **surroundings of Marrakech until about 30km**. The L25 bus goes from Marrakech to the Ourika village (25km before the valley with the Ourika waterfalls). The L33 bus will take you to AGAFAY and the L45 bus will take you to LalaTakerkoust Dam.\n\n![itineraire-periurbain bus bus marrakech\n\n**Find Here** **[Marrakech Maps to Download & Print](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/06/29/maps-of-marrakech/)**\n\n## **7/ Inter-city Regional BUS from Marrakech to Essaouira, Agadir, Ouarzazate: Supratour and Ctm**\nBetween the cities of Morocco, the B **us network is very well developed**. The network is 5000km compared to only 2000km for the railway network. 2 companies operate buses and coaches for tourists. There are other bus companies, but their safety standards and the quality of their vehicles are much lower (no air-conditioning), so they are less recommended to tourists.\n\n**The bus stations in Marrakech are linked to the south of Morocco in Ouarzazate, Essaouira, Agadir, Tan TanTan, Dakhla and to the north of Morocco in Casablanca, Tangier, Rabat, Fez and Meknes**. The fares vary between 50 and 250dhs for the southern destinations. CTM and Supratour buses companieare air-conditioned, safe and punctual. Supratour bus station is located behind the ONCF train station. Bus station of the CM buses is a little further away from the ONCF station. It is recommended to buy your ticket the day before for a morning departure. **There is no online sale**.\n\n**There are no trains in southern Morocco**. To get from Marrakech to Essaouira, Agadir, Safi or Ouarzazate and Deserts, you must use the **buses**.\n\n![](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAASwAAAC+CAYAAACPpDKJAAAACXBIWXMAAA7EAAAOxAGVKw4bAAAD+0lEQVR4Ae3QMQEAAADCoPVPbQwfiEBhwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwIABAwYMGDBgwICBx8AAe4sAAYZ/LyYAAAAASUVORK5CYII=)\n\n#### 7.1/ BUS MARRAKECH-ESSAOUIRA\nThe CTM or Supratour buses connect the 2 cities several times a day. **The trip lasts 3 hours** and the fare of a bus ticket is between 100 and 140dhs one way. **The distance is 174km**. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n#### 7.2/ BUS MARRAKECH-AGADIR\nThe CTM or Supratour buses connect the 2 cities several times a day. **The trip lasts 4 hours** and the price of a bus ticket is between 60 and 90dhs one way. **The distance is 240km**. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n#### 7.3/ BUS MARRAKECH-OUARZAZATE\nThe CTM or Supratour buses connect the 2 cities several times a day. **The trip lasts 4h15** and the price of a bus ticket is between 75 and 130dhs one way. It is a mountain road. **The distance is 220km**. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n#### 7.4/ BUS MARRAKECH-MERZOUGA\nThe CTM or Supratour buses connect the 2 cities several times a day. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n#### 7.5/ BUS MARRAKECH-ZAGORA\n**The CTM or Supratour buses** connect the 2 cities several times a day. The trip lasts 7 hours and 10 minutes and the cost of a bus ticket is between 150 and 225dhs one way. It is partly a mountain road. The distance is 380km. There is 1 stop. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n![](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAASwAAADICAYAAABS39xVAAAACXBIWXMAAA7EAAAOxAGVKw4bAAAELElEQVR4Ae3QgQAAAACAoP2pFymECgMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGDBgwYMCAAQMGBqp1AAH+Qhk2AAAAAElFTkSuQmCC)\n\n**Bus station**: Bab Doukkala, Marrakech (NOT PICK UP). Train ticket fares for a one-way train ticket from Marrakech to Casablanca cost between 95 and 130dhs in 2nd class and 130 to 190dhs in 1st class.         "markdown": "![...](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n## Our BLOG\n\n# How to get around Marrakech \\| Bus, Taxi, Horse drawn carriage, Airport shuttle, Train station, Bicycle, Car rental\n\n[**16** Oct](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/10/16/)\n\nPosted by [RiadAlKsar](https://www.alksar.com/en/author/riadalksar/)[Practical Information](https://www.alksar.com/en/category/practical-information/)[No Comments](https://www.alksar.com/en/get-around-marrakech-bus-taxi-airport-transfer-train-station/#comments)\n\n# How to Get Around Marrakech Bus Taxi Airport Transfer Train Station\n\n… Find below all the information you need to know about how to **GET AROUND MARRAKECH** bus taxi airport transfer train station, easily and cheaply. To avoid unpleasant surprises, it is strongly recommended that you know the following useful tips before venturing into the streets of the red city.\n\n**In the city, walk, use taxi, take a ride in a horse-drawn carriage, bus or bicycle** without worries.\n\nBelow you will also find the best easy, reliable and economical **transport options to REACH THE OTHER CITIES of the Kingdom, such as Casablanca, [Essaouira](https://www.alksar.com/en/marrakech-morocco-travel-tourism/excursions/essaouira-morocco/) or Tangier or the international airport Marrakech Menara, by train, bus or car.**\n\n![](https://www.alksar.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/RAK-se-deplacer-dans-marrakech-en-caleche-300x150.jpg)\n\n## 1/ From the Airport Marrakech Menara – reach the city centre by Taxi , Bus or PrivateAirport Transfer\n\nYou can **reach quickly the city center** of Marrakech from the airport: Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station. Only **4km away** from the city center. **[Airport Transfers By Reservation](https://www.alksar.com/en/airport-transfer/) or taxis** are the easiest and quickest way, especially with luggage. You have to negotiate the taxi fare. The official rate is 70dhs, but given the waiting and the airport surcharge, you will hardly pay less than 150dhs. The price from the airport to the Palmeraie is officially 100dhs, alsoto negotiate.\n\n**Bus 19 connects Menara International Airport to the city centre**, Gueliz and Medina (Arset El Bilk). The price is special and is about to 20dhs per way. There is a departure every 20 to 30 minutes, between 7am and 9:30pm. You will be at your destination in 20 minutes, the airport is only 5 km from the city centre. Purchase of the Bus Ticket on board. Single fare and round-trip fares available.\n\n**Warning**: Line 19 is in loop tour Aéroport-Medina-Gueliz. This means that by returning to the airport from the Medina (Arset El Bilk), the bus passes through Gueliz, so **it is necessary to allow more time** than the direct airport-medina arrival.\n\n**Bus 11** also passes on the avenue in front of the airport and joins the city centre and the Medina (Arset El Bilk).\n\n**Outside Gueliz and the medina**, it is recommended to use airport transfer services or taxis.\n\n**Private Airport transfers** are also a very practical way to reach your place of stay in Marrakech: personalized, from door to door, fast and safe – get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n**Read the Article** **[Marrakech Travel Advice](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/02/12/key-information-on-morocco-and-marrakech/)**\n\n## 2/ The Medina by foot\n\n**The Medina**, with its narrow car free alleyways **can be discovered by foot**. You need 30 to 40 minutes of walking to cross the medina from south to north… without getting lost. If you get lost, avoid asking a man on the street for direction. It is better to ask a woman or a shopkeeper who cannot leave her shop.\n\n**False guides** are numerous, it is a more or less illegal activity but very much practiced in Marrakech. It will be difficult for you not to be approached when you are on the street. Do not show that you are lost or be ready to pay a few dirhams if you want to be accompanied. Attention: pedestrian traffic is not a priority, so walk to the right in the medina, the mopeds in the middle.\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n**Read the Article about [Famous Jemaa El Fna Big Square](https://www.alksar.com/en/jemaa-el-fnaa-square-marrakech/)**\n\nA distinction must be made between small and large taxis\n\n## 3/By Petit Taxis in Marrakech up to 3 persons\n\n#### **Negotiate the fare before** boarding\n\nThe majority of Little taxis(sandycolor) are now Dacia Logan, formerly of the 205 Peugeot. It is possible to ride **up to 3 people**. Beyond that you will have to use the large taxis (6 or 7 people) or the horse carriage (4 to 5 people). Small taxis are officially metered. The minimum rate is 7dhs and then per 1,7 dhs for the day and 2.55dhs for the night, i. e. a legal increase of 50% from 9pm to 6am.\n\nYou will soon realize that drivers are always **trying to keep the meter off**. They will apply their package to you.\n\n**It is IMPERATIVE to negotiate the fare before sitting in a taxi**. A Medina- gueliz journey costs about 15dhs up to 3 people without luggage. Negotiate **between 20 and 30dhs during the day**, 50dhs the maximum night between Gueliz and the medina, it is generous. If the taxi doesn’t accept, wait for the next one.\n\nIf there is already a customer in the taxi, the taxi driver is still likely to stop in front of you. You will have to be careful with the rate of the meter applied if it is already on.\n\nSmall taxis travel **up to 15km from Marrakech**. Beyond that, you will have to use a large taxi.\n\n#### Green Taxis\n\nThere is a company of small taxis: **Green Taxis** 0524 40 94 99. This company charges a surcharge of 10dhs per day and 15dhs per night at the public rate. Please have the meter connected. Beyond the city centre, a flat rate of 100dhs per day is applied.\n\nGreen taxis can come to pick you up at a precise address, with some discrepancies of punctuality from time to time.\n\nJust so you know, little taxis have a number on their doors. It is necessary to note it in case of big problems, in addition to the day and time, but in general everything goes very well once the question of the fare is raised.Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n**Read Article** [**Weather & Temperatures by Month in Marrakech**](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/05/08/weather-temperatures-per-month-in-marrakech/)\n\n![Riad Al Ksar Hotel of Charm Swimming pool Marrakech medina](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nRiad Al Ksar Hotel of Charm Swimming pool Marrakech medina\n\n**[VISIT RIAD AL KSAR & SPA, Near Jemaa El Fnaa Square & Bahia Palace, in the Medina](https://www.alksar.com/en/hotel-rooms/luxury-hotel/)**\n\n## 4/By grand Taxis with 4 or more or outside Marrakech\n\nThe large taxis, formerly Mercedes, 6 seats are being replaced little by little byLodgy Daciacars 7 seats. They do not have a meter and **usually travel outside (>15km) of Marrakech**. Large taxis can be privatized. It will cost you about 700dhs for a return excursion to the valley of the Ourika and about 1000dhs for a day trip to Essaouira. For a place near the village of TninOurika (30km before the Ourika valley), the price is about 25dhs, but it is better to reserve 2 places in the front or back of the Mercedes to be more comfortable. The majority of large taxis **are parked between the mosque of Kasbah (Saadian tombs) and Djemââ el Fna square**.\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n## 5/ Horse drawn carriage in the New City of Marrakech and around the Medina ramparts\n\nThe horse drawn carriage is the means of tourist transport par excellence of Marrakech. It is very pleasant to drive over the other vehicles and to the sound of the hooves, letting time pass slowly. The carriage has 4 or 5 seats. The rates are about 150dhs for 1 hour. It is preferable to check your watch at the start. **The tour of the ramparts is done in 1h30** and the circuit of the palm grove within 3h. There is also a **45-minute tour that goes from the Royal Palace to the Hivernage, passing in front of the wall and Bab Agnaou**, the oldest gate of the medina of Marrakech, in front of the royal residence of Mohamed 6. To go from the **medina to the Majorelle garden, count 50dhs** to go by horse drawn carriage.\n\nThe majority of the horse drawncarriages are parked next to the Square Djemaa el Fna, along Foucault Square, opposite the old Club Mediterranee of the medina. Otherwise there are a few horsesdrawn carriages parked near the big hotels, near the Bahia palaces and Badia palaces, near the Majorelle garden. Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station – Attention: The horse drawncarriages do not circulate much in the medina because of the narrow streets.\n\n![visite-de-marrakech](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nvisite-de-marrakech\n\n![sejour riad piscine spa marrakech](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nsejour riad piscine spa marrakech\n\n**Don’t Miss a [TRADITIONNAL HAMMAM & MASSAGE FOR COUPLES in Marrakech Medina](https://www.alksar.com/en/spa-packages-marrakech/hammam-massage-medina/)**\n\n## 6/ Bus in the centre of Marrakech\n\nThe Marrakech Bus network is operated by ALSA [http://www.alsa.ma/fr/marrakech](http://www.alsa.ma/fr/marrakech), present throughout Morocco. The fare of the **bus ticket is 4 dhs and can be bought on board**. There is an IKLASS card to be recharged and which allows you to pay 3.50dhs per ticket. Buses are comfortable and sometimes air-conditioned. It is one more way to get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station.\n\nIt is possible to travel by bus, from the **bus station** to the Medina (Arset El Bilk, next to Jemaa el Fna) with **buses number 8 or 10**.\n\n**Bus number 1, very practical for tourists**, connects the Medina and the new city.The stops are: Djemaa el Fna Square (Arset El Bilk) in front of the square Foucault (between the place Djemaa el Fna and the mosque of the Moutoubia), **Gueliz la Grande Poste** (Place du 16 novembre), GuelizCarre Eden and it passes near the Majorelle garden and the Bahia/Badi palaces.\n\n![reseau bus marrakech](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nreseau bus marrakech\n\nSince the COP 22 in 2016, the city of Marrakech has been equipped **with”high service level” (BHNS) electric buses**. **4 Lines are planned with frequencies between 5 and 10 minutes in specific lanes.**\n\n**Buses starting with L (L22…)** are the suburban buses that serve the **surroundings of Marrakech until about 30km**. The L25 bus goes from Marrakech to the Ourika village (25km before the valley with the Ourika waterfalls). The L33 bus will take you to AGAFAY and the L45 bus will take you to LalaTakerkoust Dam.\n\n![itineraire-periurbain bus marrakech](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nitineraire-periurbain bus marrakech\n\n**Find Here** **[Marrakech Maps to Download & Print](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/06/29/maps-of-marrakech/)**\n\n## **7/ Inter-city Regional BUS from Marrakech to Essaouira, Agadir, Ouarzazate: Supratour and Ctm**\n\nBetween the cities of Morocco, the B **us network is very well developed**. The network is 5000km compared to only 2000km for the railway network. 2 companies operate buses and coaches for tourists. There are other bus companies, but their safety standards and the quality of their vehicles are much lower (no air-conditioning), so they are less recommended to tourists.\n\n**The bus stations in Marrakech are linked to the south of Morocco in Ouarzazate, Essaouira, Agadir, Tan TanTan, Dakhla and to the north of Morocco in Casablanca, Tangier, Rabat, Fez and Meknes**. The fares vary between 50 and 250dhs for the southern destinations.  CTM and Supratour buses companieare air-conditioned, safe and punctual. Supratour bus station is located behind the ONCF train station. Bus station of the CM buses is a little further away from the ONCF station. It is recommended to buy your ticket the day before for a morning departure. **There is no online sale**.\n\n**There are no trains in southern Morocco**. To get from Marrakech to Essaouira, Agadir, Safi or Ouarzazate and Deserts, you must use the **buses**.\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n#### 7.1/ BUS MARRAKECH-ESSAOUIRA\n\nThe CTM or Supratour buses connect the 2 cities several times a day. **The trip lasts 3 hours** and the fare of a bus ticket is between 100 and 140dhs one way. **The distance is 174km**. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n#### 7.2/ BUS MARRAKECH-AGADIR\n\nThe CTM or Supratour buses connect the 2 cities several times a day. **The trip lasts 4 hours** and the price of a bus ticket is between 60 and 90dhs one way. **The distance is 240km**. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n#### 7.3/ BUS MARRAKECH-OUARZAZATE\n\nThe CTM or Supratour buses connect the 2 cities several times a day. **The trip lasts 4h15** and the price of a bus ticket is between 75 and 130dhs one way. It is a mountain road. **The distance is 220km**. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n#### 7.4/ BUS MARRAKECH-MERZOUGA\n\nThe CTM or Supratour buses connect the 2 cities several times a day. The trip lasts 12h30 and the fare of a bus ticket is between 200 and 250dhs one way. It is **partly a mountain road**. **The distance is 650km**. There are 3 stops. Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n#### 7.5/ BUS MARRAKECH-ZAGORA\n\n**The CTM or Supratour buses** connect the 2 cities several times a day. The trip lasts 7 hours and 10 minutes and the cost of a bus ticket is between 150 and 225dhs one way. It is partly a mountain road. The distance is 380km. There is 1 stop.  Departures are near the ONCF train station.\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n**Bus station**: Bab Doukkala, Marrakech (NOT PICK UP). Phone: +212 (0)524 44 83 28\n\n**CTM**: 12 bdZerktouni, gueliz, Marrakech. Phone: +212 (0)524 44 74 74 20\n\n**SUPRATOUR**: avenue Hassan II, gueliz, Marrakech. Phone: +212 (0)524 43 55 25\n\n**Read the Article** [**Different Districts of Marrakech**](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/02/18/areas-of-marrakech-gueliz-medina-palmeral/)\n\n## 8/ TRAINS from Marrakech to Casablanca, Fes (Meknes, Oujda) and Tangier (Tetouan)\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n**The ONCF train station in Marrakech only serves Casablanca** and then northern Morocco. It is located in the new city of Gueliz, at the corner of Hassan 2 and Mohamed 6 avenues. **There is a train departing every 2 hours** between 5am and 24h to Casablanca. From there, you have to borrow a connection for the other northern cities. Southern Morocco is not served by train. Train schedules in Morocco are available at www.oncf.ma There is a first and second class.\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n#### 8.1/ TRAIN MARRAKECH-CASABLANCA\n\n**Casablanca, about 200km from Marrakech, is at 3h am by train**. Train ticket fares for a one-way train ticket from Marrakech to Casablanca cost between 95 and 130dhs in 2nd class and 130 to 190dhs in 1st class. The **Inter-city bus, using the motorway**, is a little cheaper and just as efficient. Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station\n\n#### 8.2/ TRAIN MARRAKECH-FES\n\nTo go from **Marrakech to Fez**, you have to change to Casablanca. **The trip lasts 8 hours**. The prices of a Train Ticket in 1st or 2nd class vary between 240 and 410dhs one way. There are buses from Marrakech to Fez, the fares are between 160 and 260dhs. A new Air Arabia flight has just been inaugurated at the end of 2017 between Marrakech and Fez. From 250dhs the seat.\n\n#### 8.3/ TRAIN MARRAKECH – TANGER\n\nFor a **Marrakech Tangier**, it is preferable to take the **direct sleeper night train** because the journey takes about 10.5 hours. There’s only one-night train a day. Day trains are possible by changing in Casablanca. The Rates of a night train ticket for a Marrakech Tangier vary between 250dhs and 470dhs. There are 18 stops. The bus Marrakech Tangier costs between 210dhs and 320dhs for a journey of 10h30 also.Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station\n\nFlights by direct plane Marrakech Tangier do not yet exist, it is necessary to change in Casablanca.\n\n#### 8.4/ FUTURE HIGH-SPEED TRAIN LGV MOROCCO\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nMorocco invests in **high-speed rail (250 km/h and more)**. The **Tangier Casablanca** line is **now completed**. In a few years (around 2028) Marrakech will be connected to the high-speed network on the Agadir – Casablanca – Tanger Train Line via Essaouira. **The Agadir-Marrakech route, planned in a few years in 1h30,** instead of 4h by bus today is already in the planning phase.\n\n**The 1st TGV (High Speed Train) of Africa! Travel in 1h30** Casablanca to Tangier, instead of 4h before. Also, there is talk (horizon 2030/2035) of a North Maghreb line that would serve Tangier – Oujda-Algiers – Tunis – Tripoli (information ONCF enterprises – master plan). The future of the train in North Africa is already underway. The lengthy journey times of the current train will then be seen as an ancestor.\n\n![Morocco High Speed train projet LGV maroc ](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nMorocco High Speed train projet LGV maroc\n\n![Cascades d'Ouzoud Maroc | Riad Al Ksar](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nCascades d’Ouzoud Maroc \\| Riad Al Ksar\n\n**DISCOVER the LIST of [DAILY EXCURSIONS AROUND MARRAKECH](https://www.alksar.com/en/marrakech-morocco-travel-tourism/excursions/)**\n\n## 9/ Car rental, Scooter, Motorcycle rental\n\n#### 9.1/ CAR RENTAL\n\nIf you wish to stroll quietly outside of Marrakech, towards Essaouira, or off the beaten tracks towards the Zat Valley, the Desert of Agafay, or the road of Tin Mal, rent a car is ideal.\n\nCar rentals are on the order of **300 to 500 dhs per day, for the 4×4 cars it will be necessary to pay approximately 1000dhs per day**, with insurance and air conditioning included.\n\nA little sporty in Marrakech, driving requires an addiction. For example, the 2 wheels pass on your right, the right priority is still very frequent, but above all it is the first one to pass. The medina is NOT accessible by car\n\nDriving on national roads is easier after leaving the city. However, you must **be extremely vigilant at night** because other vehicles do not always have light, can drive very slowly or pedestrians or bicycles can drive along the road without signs.\n\nOutside Marrakech, the **speed limits must be strictly observed**. The cops are equipped with binoculars and radars. There are also **frequent roadblocks set up** by the Royal Gendarmerie. It is absolutely necessary to slow down or even stop. The Constable will give you a signal to stop by. If you get arrested without any obvious driving fault, plan 1 or 2x100dh tickets that should fix the deal. Always remain VERY calm and smiling…. there is Always a solution in Morocco.\n\n#### 9.2/ RENTAL OF SCOOTER, MOTORCYCLE, MOTORCYCLE\n\nScooters can be rented in the city. Usually of 50cm3, they allow you to mop 1 or 2 in the vicinity of the city center. **The driver must always wear his helmet**. The rear passenger doesn’t. The policy is nowadays quite intractable on helmet wearing and insurance.\n\n**Many scooters are available for rent along Mohamed 5 Avenue between the medina and Gueliz**. Rate of about 300dhs per day.\n\nMotorcycles with a large displacement are quite expensive. Count between 600 and 900dhs the day of hiring for a 600cm3. Motorcycles are available at specialized rental agencies. Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station\n\n**[Discover the Activities to Do in Marrakech and Around](https://www.alksar.com/en/marrakech-morocco-travel-tourism/activities-quad-bike-hot-air-balloon-camel-ride/)**\n\n## 10/ Bicycle rental, Segway, Mobilboard\n\n#### BIKE SHARING RENTAL\n\nSince the COP22 World Climate Conference in November 2016, bike rentals have been available on a timeshare basis. **Medina Bike Marrakech** [https://medinabike.ma/en](https://medinabike.ma/en) (via the company SMOOVE) offers about 10n car parks (Koutoubia, Majorelle garden, train station oncf) distributed in the city where it is possible to use one of the 300 bicycles **30mn free of charge** for a subscription. Bicycles can be placed in a different parking lot from the collection point. After 30mn, each additional half hour is charged 10dhs.\n\nThe subscription fee is **50dhs for 24h rental**, 150dhs for 7 days of rental and 500dhs for 1 year of rental. This formula is very practical to discover the city center of Marrakech. This is an economic option to get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station.\n\nYou can rent your bike on the internet by obtaining a bicycle withdrawal code or from a parking agent. The advantage is that you don’t have to worry about the bike when you don’t use it and move quickly from one point to another without having to use a taxi. Moreover, Marrakech is relatively flat (slightly uphill from Gueliz to the medina towards the Atlas) so it is easy to get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n#### 10.1/ CLASSIC BIKE, MOUNTAIN BIKING\n\nGet around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station ,it is also possible to rent **personal classic bicycles or mountain bikes by day** if you wish to ride in periods of more than 30 minutes. The rental is about **150dhs for the day** or about 80dhs for the half day. Also, They must be parked in the numerous car parks in the city, easily recognizable and present at every corner of the street. A bicycle parking costs a few dirhams (2dhs per hour, 5dhs per night…)\n\n#### 10.2/ SEGWAY, MOBILBOARD\n\nSegway and Mobilboard are also available. You will easily find them along the pavements of Mohamed 5 avenue between the medina and gueliz. Prices vary. About 30 bucks a day.Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station\n\n**Read Article About the [Koutoubia Mosque, Symbol of Marrakech](https://www.alksar.com/en/koutoubia-mosque-marrakech-morocco/)**\n\n## **11/  T** **ouristic Bus**\n\nIt serves **2 loops: a “Monumental Tour” for Historic Marrakech (Main Monuments), an “Oasis” Tour by the Palmeraie and the Majorelle Garden**. The service has been relaunched in october 2017 with **electric buses**. Price is **150dhs for 24h ticket**, with free drop on drop off, and 180dhs for 48h ticket. Comments are available through headphones in the bus in 8 languages. There is a stop for Bahia Palace and Badia Palace, close to Riad Al Ksar & Spa.\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n## 12/ Avoiding Transport Scams\n\nThe bulk of the scams are about taxi fares. **Negotiate BEFORE getting IN TAXI** or horse carriage. Worst-case scenario, know that each taxi has a number on its front doors. Be sure to note this in case of problems, as well as the date and time because several drivers use the same taxi. However, we must avoid going to the police station anyway and rather “prevent rather than cure” in view of the “slowness” and detours of the administration.\n\nGet around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station is easy.For rentals, please make sure that you collect and check the rental contract and its specific conditions. Always negotiate the rate BEFOREhand and do not change the plans. **Take pictures of the vehicle** before and after use. Avoid altering anything in the rental contract without prior agreement of the lessor (duration, km etc.). As well a **lways leave a rental car in an official guarded parking lot**. Always rent a vehicle in an office and never on the street and **NEVER pay money without a receipt / invoice** or written commitment from the owner.\n\n![Spa & Riad Marrakech Al Ksar](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\nSpa & Riad Marrakech Al Ksar\n\n**[VISIT Le SPA by Riad Al Ksar: DUO HAMMAM & MASSAGES](https://www.alksar.com/en/spa-packages-marrakech/)** **In the Medina of Marrakech, Near Bahia Palace & Jemaa El Fnaa Square**\n\n## 13 / Transport safety\n\nTransport in Marrakech is often impressive, but the vehicles are finally slower than in Europe, so accidents are often very easy. Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station, the funny situations you will witness (4 on a moped) are unfortunately more tragic than 2 when a problem occurs. We therefore advise you not to try to do so and to remain vigilant at all times, especially on fast roads where collisions are unfortunately much more dramatic. It should be noted that **the risk of road traffic accidents is generally 10 times higher than in United Kingdom**! **Always be cautious… at traffic lights, stoplights and crossings** as well as when approaching another vehicle with uncertain or improbable driving behavior.\n\n**![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)**\n\n## 14/ Ecological Travel\n\nMarrakech has moved to the Electric Buses, and the Bike Sharing in open access. The good intentions expressed at the **COP22 Climate Conference in 2016** are making their way, but there is still much to be done about pollution in the city. When travelling in Marrakech, you will sometimes find it difficult to tolerate the smoke of vehicles in medina or Gueliz. Get around marrakech bus taxi airport transfer train station – The cloud of pollution is often visible above the city, as in the large western cities unfortunately. Little by little, efforts are being made. We hope to see the tramway in Marrakech soon, as is already the case in Casablanca and Rabat, and the **High Speed Train LGV, limiting the carbon footprint** of the journeys made today by taxi or airplane.\n\n![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n**In Marrakech [Stay in a Riad, like Riad Al Ksar & Spa, in the Centre of the Medina](https://www.alksar.com/en/), near Jemaa El Fnaa & the Souks**\n\n**You might also be interested in:**\n\n**[Marrakech Travel Advice](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/02/12/key-information-on-morocco-and-marrakech/)**\n\n[**Weather & Temperatures by Month in Marrakech**](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/05/08/weather-temperatures-per-month-in-marrakech/)\n\n[**Things to Do in Marrakech ?**](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/05/08/visit-marrakech/)\n\n**[Maps of Marrakech](https://www.alksar.com/en/2017/06/29/maps-of-marrakech/)**\n\n**[Marrakech Blog Homepage](https://www.alksar.com/en/blog-2/)**\n\n**©alksar 2019 – https://www.alksar.com**\n\n**Copyright Disclaimer:** USE NOTICE : This site contains copyrighted graphic material the use of which has not always been specifically authorized by the copyright owner. We are making such material (images) available in an effort to advance understanding of cultural and social subjects, etc. We believe this constitutes a ‘fair use’ of any such copyrighted graphic material.\n\n### Similar Articles :\n\n[Maps of Marrakech \\| To Print & Download](https://www.alksar.com/en/maps-of-marrakech/) [Cheap Flights to Morocco \\| Best Tips for Cheap Tickets to Marrakech](https://www.alksar.com/en/cheap-flights-to-morocco/) [Discover New Airport Terminal Marrakech Menara](https://www.alksar.com/en/marrakech-unveils-new-airport-terminal-marrakech-menara/) [Key Information on Morocco and Marrakech \\| Essentials](https://www.alksar.com/en/key-information-on-morocco-and-marrakech/) [1:54 International Contemporary African Art Fair Marrakech](https://www.alksar.com/en/154-contemporary-african-art-fair-marrakech/) [From Marrakech to Merzouga \\| 3 Day Sahara Desert Tour in Morroco](https://www.alksar.com/en/from-marrakech-to-merzouga/)\n\nManage consent\n\nTo provide the best experiences, we use technologies like cookies to store and/or access device information.
