        "url": "https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/getting-around/",
        "title": "Getting Around Morocco 2026: Trains, Buses, Taxis, Flights",
        "description": "## What's the best way to get around Morocco?\nIt depends on the route. ONCF trains are excellent for the Casablanca-Marrakech-Tangier corridor. CTM buses cover most cities reliably. For the south (Sahara, Draa Valley), car rental or an organised tour is most practical. Petit taxis handle city travel. Domestic flights save time on very long routes.\n\n## At a glance\n|  |  |\n|-|-|\n| Best for the north | ONCF trains — Casablanca, Rabat, Marrakech, Tangier corridor |\n| Best for the south | Car rental or an [organised Sahara tour](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/how-to-book-sahara-tour/) — trains don’t reach [Merzouga](https://www.morocco-escape.com/destinations/merzouga/) or [Zagora](https://www.morocco-escape.com/destinations/zagora/) |\n| Typical city taxi fare | Around 10-25 MAD for a short petit taxi ride |\n| Booking lead time | 24-48h ahead for ONCF/CTM in peak season; longer around holidays |\n| Getting oriented | The [Morocco trip planning guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/plan-your-trip/) covers how transport ties into an itinerary |\n\n## Morocco’s transport options: what works, what doesn’t\nMorocco has a better transport network than many travellers expect. The ONCF rail system runs clean, punctual trains on the main northern corridor. Long-distance buses connect most cities. In Marrakech and Fes, taxis are cheap and reliable for city movement.\n\nThe gap is the south. Once you’re heading for the Sahara, Atlas Mountains, or the Draa Valley, public transport becomes sparse and organised tours or car rental become the practical options.\n\nThis guide covers every mode of transport with realistic information about cost, reliability, and where each option works best.\n\n## Long-distance buses: CTM and Supratours\n### CTM (Compagnie de Transports au Maroc)\n- **Marrakech → Agadir:** 3h30, ~100-130 MAD\n- **Marrakech → Casablanca:** 3h30, ~90-120 MAD (though train is often faster and similar price)\n- **Marrakech → Fes:** 8-9h, ~180-220 MAD\n- **Marrakech → Zagora:** 6-7h, ~120-150 MAD\n- **Fes → Chefchaouen:** 4h, ~100 MAD\n- **Fes → Tangier:** 5h, ~120 MAD\n\n## Taxis: petit vs grand\n### Petit taxis\nSmall metered city taxis for urban movement within a single city. Each city has its own colour (red in Marrakech, blue in Fes, yellow in Casablanca). They seat 3 passengers and meters are required.\n\n**In practice:** In Marrakech especially, drivers sometimes claim the meter is broken or try to negotiate a fixed price before you get in. Get in, point to the meter, and say “conteur” (counter). If the driver refuses to use the meter, get out and find another taxi. Typical fares within Marrakech: 10-25 MAD for short to medium journeys.\n\nDrivers in major cities often speak some French and occasionally basic English.\n\n## Domestic flights\nRoyal Air Maroc (RAM) and Air Arabia Maroc connect Morocco’s cities with multiple daily frequencies on main routes. For travellers whose primary destination is the desert, an organised tour handles all transport logistics in one booking — the [3-day Sahara desert trip from Marrakech](https://www.getyourguide.com/marrakesh-l208/from-marrakech-3-day-sahara-desert-trip-to-merzouga-t56040/?partner_id=LDXYA0P&cmp=morocco-escape&utm_medium=online_publisher) is the most common approach.\n\nThe [Marrakech to Fes via Merzouga 3-day tour](https://www.getyourguide.com/marrakesh-l208/marrakech-to-fez-via-merzouga-desert-3-days-sahara-tour-t487201/?partner_id=LDXYA0P&cmp=morocco-escape&utm_medium=online_publisher) handles the one-way routing as a single transfer, eliminating the need to manage transport between the two gateway cities independently. Useful routes:\n\n- **Casablanca → Marrakech:** 50min flight vs 3h train; cost 60-150 EUR vs 15-20 EUR train — the flight only makes sense if time is extremely tight\n- **Casablanca → Agadir:** 1h flight; no train equivalent; comparable to the bus on time but much more expensive\n- **Marrakech → Casablanca:** Rarely worth it unless connecting to international flight with tight connection\n- **Casablanca → Fes:** 1h flight; the train is nearly as fast and much cheaper\n\nIn general, Morocco is compact enough that domestic flights rarely make sense unless you’re specifically transferring to international connection or have very limited time. The train from Marrakech to Casablanca airport (CMN) for an international connection is the most common sensible use case.\n\nBook at royalairmaroc.com or through comparison sites.\n\n## City-specific transport notes\n**Marrakech:** Petit taxis for everything within the city. Marrakech is walkable within the medina (though complex); the new city (Gueliz) is 20 minutes walk from the medina or a short taxi ride.\n\n**Fes:** Petit taxis essential for the new medina (Fes el-Jdid) to old medina (Fes el-Bali) transfer and to Bou Jeloud gate. Inside the medina itself, everything is on foot.\n\n**Casablanca:** The city has a tram network (Al Bidaoui) covering the main urban axis — useful for avoiding traffic. Taxis are also plentiful.\n\n**Chefchaouen:** Small enough to walk everywhere. No taxis needed within the medina.\n\n## Frequently asked questions about getting around Morocco\n### Is Uber available in Morocco?\nIn Marrakech and Casablanca, this is genuinely useful — consistent pricing and no language barrier for destination entry.",
        "position": 3,
        "markdown": "[Skip to content](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/getting-around/#main-content)\n\n![Getting Around Morocco: Transport Guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/images/guides/getting-around.jpg)\n\nQuick answer\n\n## What's the best way to get around Morocco?\n\nIt depends on the route. ONCF trains are excellent for the Casablanca-Marrakech-Tangier corridor. CTM buses cover most cities reliably. For the south (Sahara, Draa Valley), car rental or an organised tour is most practical. Petit taxis handle city travel. Domestic flights save time on very long routes.\n\n## At a glance\n\n|  |  |\n| --- | --- |\n| Best for the north | ONCF trains — Casablanca, Rabat, Marrakech, Tangier corridor |\n| Best for the south | Car rental or an [organised Sahara tour](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/how-to-book-sahara-tour/) — trains don’t reach [Merzouga](https://www.morocco-escape.com/destinations/merzouga/) or [Zagora](https://www.morocco-escape.com/destinations/zagora/) |\n| Typical city taxi fare | Around 10-25 MAD for a short petit taxi ride |\n| Booking lead time | 24-48h ahead for ONCF/CTM in peak season; longer around holidays |\n| Getting oriented | The [Morocco trip planning guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/plan-your-trip/) covers how transport ties into an itinerary |\n\n## Morocco’s transport options: what works, what doesn’t\n\nMorocco has a better transport network than many travellers expect. The ONCF rail system runs clean, punctual trains on the main northern corridor. Long-distance buses connect most cities. In Marrakech and Fes, taxis are cheap and reliable for city movement.\n\nThe gap is the south. Once you’re heading for the Sahara, Atlas Mountains, or the Draa Valley, public transport becomes sparse and organised tours or car rental become the practical options.\n\nThis guide covers every mode of transport with realistic information about cost, reliability, and where each option works best.\n\n* * *\n\n## ONCF trains\n\nMorocco’s national rail operator (Office National des Chemins de Fer) runs one of Africa’s most functional rail networks. The core network connects:\n\n- **Casablanca ↔ Marrakech:** 3h journey, frequent services, 80-180 MAD (2nd/1st class)\n- **Casablanca ↔ Rabat:** 1h, very frequent\n- **Casablanca ↔ Tangier:** 4h30 (classic service), or 2h10 on the Al Boraq high-speed train\n- **Casablanca ↔ Fes/Meknes:** 4h to Fes via Rabat\n\n**Al Boraq high-speed train:** Launched in 2018, Morocco’s flagship TGV-style service runs between Tangier and Casablanca. At 320km/h, it covers the 350km route in 2h10. Fares vary with class and booking date; check the current price on the ONCF website. The train is clean, air-conditioned, and punctual. It makes Tangier reasonably accessible from Casablanca as a day trip.\n\n**Booking:** Online at oncf.ma or at station ticket windows. Booking 24-48h ahead is usually sufficient except public holidays. First class (around double the price) is worth it for longer journeys — wider seats, better air conditioning.\n\n**Key limitation:** The rail network does not extend to Marrakech-Agadir, Fes-Merzouga, or any of the southern circuits. For destinations south of Marrakech, trains aren’t the answer.\n\n* * *\n\n## Long-distance buses: CTM and Supratours\n\n### CTM (Compagnie de Transports au Maroc)\n\nCTM is Morocco’s premier bus operator — air-conditioned, usually punctual, assigned seats, luggage storage underneath. The network is extensive:\n\n- **Marrakech → Agadir:** 3h30, ~100-130 MAD\n- **Marrakech → Casablanca:** 3h30, ~90-120 MAD (though train is often faster and similar price)\n- **Marrakech → Fes:** 8-9h, ~180-220 MAD\n- **Marrakech → Zagora:** 6-7h, ~120-150 MAD\n- **Fes → Chefchaouen:** 4h, ~100 MAD\n- **Fes → Tangier:** 5h, ~120 MAD\n\nBook at ctm.ma or at the CTM station (separate from the main bus gare routière in most cities).\n\n### Supratours\n\nONCF’s bus subsidiary covering routes that complement the rail network — particularly Marrakech to Agadir and to Essaouira. Generally comparable quality to CTM. Useful for Essaouira (Marrakech → Essaouira: 3h, ~100 MAD).\n\n### Standard (gare routière) buses\n\nCheaper than CTM/Supratours but less comfortable, no assigned seats, schedules less reliable. For budget travellers, these are functional for many routes. Buy at the gare routière (main bus station) in person.\n\n* * *\n\n## Taxis: petit vs grand\n\n### Petit taxis\n\nSmall metered city taxis for urban movement within a single city. Each city has its own colour (red in Marrakech, blue in Fes, yellow in Casablanca). They seat 3 passengers and meters are required.\n\n**In practice:** In Marrakech especially, drivers sometimes claim the meter is broken or try to negotiate a fixed price before you get in. Get in, point to the meter, and say “conteur” (counter). If the driver refuses to use the meter, get out and find another taxi. Typical fares within Marrakech: 10-25 MAD for short to medium journeys.\n\nDrivers in major cities often speak some French and occasionally basic English.\n\n### Grand taxis\n\nShared long-distance taxis operating specific routes, carrying up to 6 passengers (the front seat takes 2 people besides the driver — it’s tight). They depart when full and cover routes that buses don’t serve, particularly between smaller towns.\n\nFares are fixed per route and cheap — sometimes the fastest option for a specific town-to-town journey. Ask at your accommodation what the standard fare is before getting in.\n\nGrand taxis are also available for private hire. Negotiating a private grand taxi for a half-day excursion can be excellent value (200-400 MAD for 3-4 hours depending on distance).\n\n* * *\n\n## Car rental\n\n### When it makes sense\n\nCar rental gives Morocco at a different level of freedom — pull over for an Atlas mountain view, stop at a roadside argan cooperative, take a detour to a remote kasbah without asking anyone’s permission.\n\nThe south is where car rental makes the most sense: the Draa Valley, the circuit through Ouarzazate, the Dadès Gorge, and the approach to Merzouga are all significantly better experienced at your own pace than on an operator’s schedule.\n\n### Cost\n\nRental rates vary considerably by operator and season:\n\n- Small car (Dacia Sandero / Renault Clio): 200-400 MAD/day\n- SUV (recommended for mountain roads and piste tracks): 400-700 MAD/day\n- Including CDW (collision damage waiver): add 100-150 MAD/day\n\nBook in advance through international operators (Europcar, Hertz, Avis all operate in Morocco) or reliable local operators. Local operators at the airport can be significantly cheaper but read reviews carefully.\n\n### Driving practicalities\n\n**Roads:** National routes (N) and regional routes (R) are generally paved and in reasonable condition. Mountain roads (particularly the Tizi n’Tichka and Tizi n’Test passes) are paved but narrow with significant drop-offs — require attention.\n\n**Night driving:** Strongly not recommended on mountain roads. Unlit sections, livestock on the road, and poor road edge markings make nighttime driving in the Atlas genuinely dangerous.\n\n**Speed cameras:** Automated speed cameras are common on national routes. Fines are issued to the rental company and passed back to you. Drive at or below posted limits.\n\n**Fuel:** Petrol (essence) and diesel (gasoil) available throughout the country. Remote areas have longer gaps between stations — fill up when below half tank in the south.\n\n**Parking:** In cities, paid parking attendants (gardiens) manage street spaces. Give them 5-10 MAD on return to your car. Most hotels have parking; riads often do not.\n\n**Piste tracks:** Some areas (particularly into Erg Chigaga and remote Atlas) require genuine 4WD. A standard rental car will not handle serious piste tracks — rent a 4WD or take an operator’s vehicle.\n\n* * *\n\n## Domestic flights\n\nRoyal Air Maroc (RAM) and Air Arabia Maroc connect Morocco’s cities with multiple daily frequencies on main routes. For travellers whose primary destination is the desert, an organised tour handles all transport logistics in one booking — the [3-day Sahara desert trip from Marrakech](https://www.getyourguide.com/marrakesh-l208/from-marrakech-3-day-sahara-desert-trip-to-merzouga-t56040/?partner_id=LDXYA0P&cmp=morocco-escape&utm_medium=online_publisher) is the most common approach.\n\nThe [Marrakech to Fes via Merzouga 3-day tour](https://www.getyourguide.com/marrakesh-l208/marrakech-to-fez-via-merzouga-desert-3-days-sahara-tour-t487201/?partner_id=LDXYA0P&cmp=morocco-escape&utm_medium=online_publisher) handles the one-way routing as a single transfer, eliminating the need to manage transport between the two gateway cities independently. Useful routes:\n\n- **Casablanca → Marrakech:** 50min flight vs 3h train; cost 60-150 EUR vs 15-20 EUR train — the flight only makes sense if time is extremely tight\n- **Casablanca → Agadir:** 1h flight; no train equivalent; comparable to the bus on time but much more expensive\n- **Marrakech → Casablanca:** Rarely worth it unless connecting to international flight with tight connection\n- **Casablanca → Fes:** 1h flight; the train is nearly as fast and much cheaper\n\nIn general, Morocco is compact enough that domestic flights rarely make sense unless you’re specifically transferring to international connection or have very limited time. The train from Marrakech to Casablanca airport (CMN) for an international connection is the most common sensible use case.\n\nBook at royalairmaroc.com or through comparison sites.\n\n* * *\n\n## City-specific transport notes\n\n**Marrakech:** Petit taxis for everything within the city. Marrakech is walkable within the medina (though complex); the new city (Gueliz) is 20 minutes walk from the medina or a short taxi ride.\n\n**Fes:** Petit taxis essential for the new medina (Fes el-Jdid) to old medina (Fes el-Bali) transfer and to Bou Jeloud gate. Inside the medina itself, everything is on foot.\n\n**Casablanca:** The city has a tram network (Al Bidaoui) covering the main urban axis — useful for avoiding traffic. Taxis are also plentiful.\n\n**Chefchaouen:** Small enough to walk everywhere. No taxis needed within the medina.\n\n* * *\n\n## Transport summary by route type\n\n| Journey type | Best option | Alternative |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| City to city (north) | ONCF train | CTM bus |\n| City to city (south) | CTM bus or car rental | Grand taxi |\n| Within a city | Petit taxi | Walking |\n| Sahara tour | Organised tour | Car rental + camp booking |\n| Airport transfer | Pre-booked hotel transfer or official taxi rank | Grand taxi |\n| Remote Atlas/desert | 4WD rental or tour | Grand taxi (some routes) |\n\n* * *\n\n## Practical transport tips\n\n- Book CTM and ONCF tickets at least 24h ahead for popular routes in peak season\n- Have the address of your accommodation in Arabic for taxi drivers (your riad will have a card)\n- Negotiate grand taxi fares before departure; ask your accommodation for the standard rate\n- Keep small bills (10, 20 MAD) for taxis and tips\n- Airport official taxi stands are safer than negotiating outside arrivals\n\nFor overall trip planning including how transport integrates with your itinerary, the [Morocco trip planning guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/plan-your-trip/) covers the full picture. The [Morocco budget guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/budget/) includes transport cost breakdown by travel style. For the Sahara desert specifically, the [how to book a Sahara tour guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/how-to-book-sahara-tour/) explains how transport is typically bundled into tour packages. The [Sahara from Marrakech vs Fes guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/sahara-from-marrakech-vs-fes/) compares driving routes to the desert. The [best time to visit Morocco guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/best-time-to-visit/) helps align transport planning with seasonal conditions.\n\n* * *\n\n## Frequently asked questions about getting around Morocco\n\n### Is the train from Marrakech to Casablanca reliable?\n\nONCF has a decent punctuality record by regional standards. Expect occasional 15-30 minute delays. For international flight connections, build at least 3h buffer between scheduled train arrival at Casablanca Voyageurs and your flight departure.\n\n### Can I take a bus directly from Marrakech to Merzouga?\n\nCTM does not run directly to Merzouga. The closest option is a bus to Rissani or Erfoud (via Fes or Marrakech), then a petit taxi negotiation to Merzouga (~60km). Total journey time from Marrakech: 12-14h. An organised tour or car rental is significantly more practical for the Sahara.\n\n### Is driving in Morocco difficult?\n\nIn cities, yes — Casablanca and Marrakech have aggressive traffic and limited signage. On national routes and mountain roads, it’s manageable but requires attention. Night driving on mountain roads is not recommended. For first-time visitors, getting a feeling for Moroccan driving dynamics in a smaller city before tackling Casablanca makes sense.\n\n### Do Moroccan taxi drivers speak English?\n\nMajor cities (Marrakech, Casablanca): some English. Smaller cities: usually French. Rural areas: Darija and Amazigh. Having your destination written in French or Arabic on your phone covers most situations.\n\n### Is Uber available in Morocco?\n\nCareem (acquired by Uber) operates in Morocco’s major cities. The app-based ride experience eliminates meter negotiation. In Marrakech and Casablanca, this is genuinely useful — consistent pricing and no language barrier for destination entry.\n\n### How do I get from Casablanca airport to Fes?\n\nBest option: train from Casa Voyageurs (take the shuttle train from the airport to Casa Port, then transfer). Journey to Fes runs approximately 4h. The CTM bus option is also available from Casablanca city. A direct airport taxi to Fes (200-250 EUR) is expensive but convenient if arriving late with luggage.\n\n### Should I hire a private driver instead of self-organising transport?\n\nFor a first trip covering several regions in a short window, a private driver removes a lot of the friction covered above — no ticket booking, no grand taxi negotiation, no night driving. It costs more than public transport but less than most people expect for a small group. The [private tour vs self-drive guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/private-tour-vs-self-drive-morocco/) and the [should you use a private driver in Morocco blog post](https://www.morocco-escape.com/blog/should-you-use-a-private-driver-in-morocco/) both weigh this against car rental and organised tours in more detail.\n\n* * *\n\n## Getting around within the medinas\n\nThe historic medinas of Marrakech, Fes, Chefchaouen, and other Moroccan cities are navigated exclusively on foot. No vehicles enter the main medina lanes (though motorbikes sometimes do in Fes, which is a hazard in narrow alleys — listen for them).\n\n**Key medina navigation tips:**\n\nIn Marrakech, the medina is divided by the main souk axis running north from Djemaa el-Fna. The Bab Doukkala gate (north-west), Bab Debbagh (east), and Bab Agnaou (south) are the main entry/exit points. Your riad will send you a pin or a card — save the location on your phone.\n\nIn Fes, the two main orientation anchors are Bab Bou Jeloud (the blue ornamental gate, western entry to Fes el-Bali) and the Chouara tanneries (eastern section of the medina). Navigating between these two points takes you through most of the main medina sights. Every other lane is a potential maze.\n\nIn Chefchaouen, the small size makes getting lost essentially impossible — the blue-painted kasbah is visible from most streets and the medina takes 15-20 minutes to walk end to end.\n\n**Motorised transport inside medinas:** Not possible for taxis. Some locals use motorbikes and donkey carts for goods transport in wider lanes. In Fes especially, donkey carts appear without warning in narrow lanes — step against the wall.\n\n* * *\n\n## Negotiating grand taxi fares\n\nGrand taxis have fixed per-seat fares for specific routes, but these are rarely posted and tourists frequently pay tourist rates.\n\nThe reliable approach:\n\n1. Ask your accommodation what the standard fare is for your destination before leaving\n2. At the taxi stand, ask for the fare before getting in — state the destination clearly\n3. If offered a significantly higher price, counter with the amount you were told\n4. For private hire (taking all 6 seats yourself), the fare is typically 5-6x the individual seat rate — negotiate from there\n\nCommon tourist mistake: agreeing to a shared grand taxi but discovering the driver won’t leave until all seats are full, then waiting 45 minutes. If time matters, pay for 2 seats to get the taxi moving (this works for couples or can be negotiated alone for 1.5-2x the single-seat fare).\n\n* * *\n\n## Crossing into Spain: the Ceuta and Melilla border\n\nFor travellers doing a Morocco-Spain combination, the Spanish enclave crossings deserve specific mention.\n\n**Ceuta crossing (Bab Sebta):** Pedestrian and vehicle crossing open 24 hours. Queue times vary enormously — busy weekends can mean 2-4 hour waits, slow days 20 minutes. EU rules apply inside Ceuta; Moroccan immigration is at the boundary. Frequent ferry services from Algeciras to Ceuta (35 minutes) and Tarifa to Tangier Med (45 minutes).\n\n**Tangier Med port:** The main car and passenger ferry terminal for vehicle crossings. Located 45km east of Tangier city — taxi or bus required between the port and city. Multiple operators run ferries to Algeciras and other Spanish ports. Journey time to Algeciras: 90 minutes.\n\n* * *\n\n## Combining transport modes on a real itinerary\n\nMost trips end up mixing two or three of these options rather than picking one. A common pattern: ONCF train for the Casablanca-Marrakech leg, then a car rental or organised tour for the Atlas/Sahara loop, then the train again for the return to the airport.\n\nThe [morocco by train itinerary](https://www.morocco-escape.com/itineraries/morocco-by-train-itinerary/) shows how far you can get on rail alone before you need to switch modes, while the [self-drive Morocco itinerary](https://www.morocco-escape.com/itineraries/self-drive-morocco-itinerary/) and [with-driver Morocco itinerary](https://www.morocco-escape.com/itineraries/with-driver-morocco-itinerary/) map out the two main alternatives for the sections trains don’t reach. If you’re deciding how much ground to cover in total, the [7 vs 10 days guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/morocco-7-vs-10-days/) and [10 vs 14 days guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/morocco-10-vs-14-days/) both factor in how much time transport itself eats into a trip.\n\n## Related guides\n\n[![3-Day Sahara Desert Tour from Marrakech: Complete Route Guide](https://www.morocco-escape.com/images/guides/3-day-sahara-tour-marrakech.jpg)\\\\\n\\\\\nGuides **3-Day Sahara Desert Tour from Marrakech: Complete Route Guide**](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/3-day-sahara-tour-marrakech/) [![Best Time to Visit Morocco](https://www.morocco-escape.com/images/guides/best-time-to-visit.jpg)\\\\\n\\\\\nGuides **Best Time to Visit Morocco**](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/best-time-to-visit/) [![Morocco Trip Cost: Complete Budget Breakdown](https://www.morocco-escape.com/images/guides/budget.jpg)\\\\\n\\\\\nGuides **Morocco Trip Cost: Complete Budget Breakdown**](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/budget/) [![25 Mistakes First-Time Visitors Make in Morocco](https://www.morocco-escape.com/images/guides/first-time-visitors.jpg)\\\\\n\\\\\nGuides **25 Mistakes First-Time Visitors Make in Morocco**](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/first-time-visitors/) [![How to Book a Sahara Desert Tour in Morocco Without Getting Ripped Off](https://www.morocco-escape.com/images/guides/how-to-book-sahara-tour.jpg)\\\\\n\\\\\nGuides **How to Book a Sahara Desert Tour in Morocco Without Getting Ripped Off**](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/how-to-book-sahara-tour/) [![Is Morocco Safe? An Honest Assessment for 2026](https://www.morocco-escape.com/images/guides/is-morocco-safe.jpg)\\\\\n\\\\\nGuides **Is Morocco Safe? An Honest Assessment for 2026**](https://www.morocco-escape.com/guides/is-morocco-safe/)",

        "url": "https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/tips-on-taking-local-city-bus-in-marrakech/",
        "title": "Marrakech's Public Transport. Taxi vs City Bus | Blondie in Morocco",
        "description": "A one-way bus ticket costs 4 MAD and can only be bought on board; it is advisable to pay in coins. The city bus routes are available on ALSA page. The price of ...",
        "position": 4,
        "markdown": "[Skip to content](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/tips-on-taking-local-city-bus-in-marrakech/#main)\n\n![](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/01/Marrakech-bus-painting.png)\n\n**Shall you use the local city bus in Marrakech? Or rather trust taxies? Some of my Moroccan friends have never used any public transport in their lives. Not because they are rich, but because taxis are almost as cheap to travel as buses.**\n\nIn Marrakech, taxis are the most comfortable public transport, and their prices are low. In addition, taxis are more convenient than overcrowded public buses, which often go without a schedule, and don’t have air conditioning.\n\nFor example, a one-way bus ticket from Carre Eden shopping mall to the Koutoubia Mosque costs 4 MAD. If the taxi meter is on for the same distance, you will pay around 10 MAD. Therefore, if you are travelling not alone, it is cheaper to take a taxi. For more about taxis in Morocco, please read my post [Taking taxi in Morocco: a survival guide](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/taking-taxi-in-morocco-guide/).\n\n![](https://www.blondinemaroke.lt/wp-content/uploads/2021/10/rasa_barcaite_blondine_maroke.webp)\n\nYour Morocco trip begins here\n\n_10 years in Morocco. Boutique planning for curious travellers_\n\n- [Complete A–Z itinerary planning](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/custom-morocco-itinerary-planning/)\n- [Personal video consultation call](https://calendly.com/blondie_in_morocco/travel-consultation-call-maxi)\n- [10 minute free discovery call](https://calendly.com/blondie_in_morocco/free-discovery-call)\n- [Desert trips & self drive itinerary](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/morocco-itineraries-tours/)\n\n[Speak with rasa](https://calendly.com/blondie_in_morocco/free-discovery-call?month=2025-12)\n\nWhere to find bus stops? At the Jemaa el-Fnaa square, the nearest bus stop is where the horse-drawn carriages stand. This stop is called Terminus Arset El Bilk, and there also stops the bus No. 19 on its way from the airport.\n\n![](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/01/marrakech-city-bus.jpg)\n\nBus No. 1 connects Medina with the Gueliz district. It stops near my recommended Ensemble Artisanal crafts centre, then follows the “Carre Eden” shopping centre, where you should get off the bus to go sightseeing in Gueliz.\n\nIf you want to visit the Majorelle Garden, take bus No. 12 or No. 15 at the Koutoubia stop (walk a few mins further from the Jemaa el-Fnaa bus stop ). Then, get off at Ben Tbib stop and walk for about 5 min till you reach the Majorelle Garden. Unfortunately, the names of the bus stops are not announced aloud, so you will have to ask for the right place to get off.\n\nA one-way bus ticket costs 4 MAD and can only be bought on board; it is advisable to pay in coins. The city bus routes are available on [ALSA page](http://www.alsa.ma/marrakech/lignes).\n\n#### Suburban buses\n\nThe ALSA buses can also take you about 30km beyond Marrakech city. Search for busses with the letter L (L45 etc.). Most of these buses start from the bus stop area called “Sidi Mimoun”, around a 10-minute walk from Jemaa el-Fnaa. If you search on Google for “Sidi Mimoun Region”, it should show you the exact bus stop place.\n\n![](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/11/marrakech-bus-alsa.jpg)\n\nWith bus No. L45, you will go to a Lala Takerkoust Dam (read my post [A paradise next to Marrakech, Lake Lalla Takerkoust](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/a-paradise-next-to-marrakech-lake-lalla-takerkoust/)). Bus No. L25 will take you to the Ourika valley, not to the famous Setti Fatma Falls, but 25km to them; you will have to go by taxi. Bus No. L 33 will take you to the Agafay desert. You will find the maps of the ALSA public bus routes [here](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/morocco-maps/). The price of a one-way ticket is 7 MAD.\n\nTo sum up, while in Marrakech, I would advise using taxis instead of local city buses. However, if you are adventurous and have time to explore, hop on one of the local buses and immerse yourself in local life. I have done it many times!\n\n## Similar Posts\n\n- [![How to get from Marrakech airport to the city center](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/03/marrakech-menara-airport2.jpg)](https://www.blondieinmorocco.com/how-to-get-from-marrakech-airport-to-the-city-center/)\n\n- [![The Celestial Staircase in Morocco Hansjorg         "title": "From Marrakesh Airport to City Center Every Option Explained 2026",
        "description": "# From _Marrakesh Airport_ to City Center — Every Option Explained\n## Overview: Marrakesh Menara Airport [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/#mrc-toc)\nMarrakesh Menara Airport (IATA: RAK) sits roughly **6 km southwest of Jemaa el-Fna square** — a surprisingly short distance that makes it one of the more accessible airport-to-city transfers in North Africa. Despite that, plenty of first-time visitors end up paying far too much simply because they don’t know which options are available and what a fair fare looks like.\n\nThe airport serves international and domestic flights, and arrivals pour out into a single terminal. Just outside the exit doors you’ll find taxis, a public bus stop, and occasional private transfer drivers holding name signs. The city center — whether you’re heading to the medina, Gueliz, or Hivernage — is typically reachable in **10 to 25 minutes** depending on traffic.\n\nBefore you travel, it’s worth understanding [what currency Morocco uses](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/what-currency-does-morocco-use/), since all local transport is paid in Moroccan dirhams (MAD). Having small change ready saves you the awkward rounding-up games that some drivers play.\n\n![Marrakesh Menara Airport exterior arrivals area](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Marrakesh-Menara-Airport-exterior-arrivals-area.jpg)\n\nThe arrivals area at Marrakesh Menara Airport — taxis and buses are directly outside.\n\n## All Transport Options at a Glance [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/#mrc-toc)\n| Option | Est. Fare (MAD) | Duration | Best For | Price Clarity |\n|-|-|-|-|-|\n| **Public Bus Line 19** | 30 MAD | ~35–50 min | Budget travelers | Fixed |\n| **Petit Taxi (metered)** | 50–100 MAD | ~12–20 min | Solo / duo travelers | Metered* |\n| **Ride-Hailing (Careem / inDrive)** | 70–100 MAD | ~12–20 min | Comfort + price visibility | App-fixed |\n| **Private Transfer** | 150–250 MAD | ~10–18 min | Families, groups, late arrivals | Pre-agreed |\n\n*Petit taxis are metered by law, but unscrupulous drivers may quote flat rates that exceed the meter. See the taxi section below for how to handle this.\n\n🚌\n\nPublic Bus Line 19\n\nCheapest option, fixed posted price. Runs regularly but takes longer due to stops.\n\n30 MAD\n\n🚕\n\nPetit Taxi\n\nFastest for solo travelers. Insist on the meter or agree upfront. Night surcharge applies.\n\n~50–100 MAD\n\n📱\n\nRide-Hailing\n\nPrice shown before you book. Available through Careem and inDrive apps.\n\n~70–100 MAD\n\n🚗\n\nPrivate Transfer\n\nDriver meets you with a sign. Pre-arranged, no negotiation, perfect for large luggage.\n\n~150–250 MAD\n\n## Marrakech Airport Taxi Price: What to Expect [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/#mrc-toc)\nThe small orange _petit taxis_ parked outside arrivals are the go-to choice for most solo travelers and couples heading into Marrakesh. By law, these taxis are equipped with meters, and the official **Marrakech airport taxi price** from the airport to Jemaa el-Fna or central Gueliz should land between **50 and 80 MAD** during the day — slightly more at night when a 50% nighttime surcharge kicks in, bringing the fare to roughly **75–100 MAD**.\n\n⚠️ Watch Out for Inflated Fares\n\nSome drivers at the airport will either “forget” to turn on the meter or quote flat rates of 150–200 MAD before you even get in. This is a common tactic aimed at freshly-landed tourists. Always ask the driver to use the meter ( _“il compteur, s’il vous plaît”_), or agree on a price before boarding. If a driver refuses to use the meter, simply walk away — there are always others. For a full breakdown of how taxis work across the city, read this practical guide on [taxi tips in Marrakech](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/taxi-tips-in-marrakech/), including how to spot a licensed driver and what to say to avoid being overcharged.\n\nIt’s also important to know the real rate before you step outside the terminal. Ask your hotel in advance, check recent traveler reports online, or simply read the tips in this guide. Being informed is the single most effective way to avoid overpaying. And if you’re wondering about general safety — [is Marrakech safe?](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/is-marrakech-safe/) — the answer for most travelers is yes, especially when you know what to look out for.\n\nNote that petit taxis cannot officially take you all the way inside the old medina’s narrow alleyways. The driver will drop you at a nearby point (like Djemaa el-Fna or a city gate called a _bab_), from where you walk a short distance.\n\n![Petit taxis waiting outside Marrakesh Menara Airport](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Marrakesh-Menara-Airport-arrivals-hall.webp)\n\nOrange petit taxis lined up at the Marrakesh airport exit.\n\n## Marrakech Airport to City Centre Bus — Line 19 [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/#mrc-toc)\nIf you’re traveling on a tight budget or simply prefer public transport, **Bus Line 19** is the only bus that connects Marrakesh Menara Airport directly to the city center. The fare is clearly posted on the bus and at the stop — one of the only transport options in Marrakesh where the price is truly fixed with no room for negotiation.\n\n✓ Fact Check — 2026 Price\n\nSeveral travel blogs and more recently published articles have started citing the Bus Line 19 fare as **50 MAD** — this is **incorrect**. As of May 2026, the correct and official price is **30 MAD**, as clearly displayed on the fare board at the airport bus stop and confirmed directly on the bus. This ticket also entitles you to a free return journey, valid for 15 days. If a conductor or driver asks for more than 30 MAD, that is not the official rate — the posted fare is what you should pay.\n\n🚌 Bus Line 19 — Key Details (May 2026)\n\nFare: **30 MAD** (includes a free return, valid 15 days) · Route: Airport → Avenue Mohammed VI → Jemaa el-Fna · Frequency: Every 20–30 minutes · Travel time: ~35–50 minutes\n\n![Public bus in Marrakech city](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Bus-Marrakech.webp)\n\nA Marrakech public bus — Bus Line 19 operates the same fleet on the airport route.\n\nLine 19Marrakech Airport Bus 19 Route Map\n\n⏱ ~35–50 min✈RAKMenara AirportDeparture point30 MADAv. Mohammed VIIntermediate stopGuélizNew town centre🏛ENDJemaa el-FnaMedina entranceEvery 20–30 minutesSW ←→ NE\n\nSimplified route diagram for Bus Line 19 — not to scale. Stops shown are principal points along the route.\n\n![Marrakech Bus Line 19 at the airport stop](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Bus-Marrakech-Line-19.jpeg)\n\nBus Line 19 at Marrakesh Menara Airport — fares are clearly displayed on the board.\n\nThe bus is a solid choice if you’re traveling light and don’t mind a journey of about 40 minutes with a few stops. It runs throughout the day but becomes less frequent in the late evening, so if you have a night arrival, verify the schedule in advance. The bus drops you near Jemaa el-Fna, making it convenient for anyone staying in the medina.\n\n🚌 Other City Bus Lines — 5 to 6 MAD\n\nYou may come across mentions of local Marrakesh bus lines that charge just **5 to 6 MAD** per journey. These exist, but there’s an important catch: **none of them serve the airport directly**. To use these cheaper city buses, you would first need to walk out of the airport grounds — a stretch of around 15–20 minutes on foot along a road with no pedestrian infrastructure — before reaching a stop on the main road where these lines run. Unless you are very comfortable with the city’s bus network and traveling with minimal luggage, **Bus Line 19 at 30 MAD remains the practical option** for airport arrivals.\n\nPay in **dirhams** directly to the driver or conductor. Having exact change is appreciated, though not always required.\n\nIn a Hurry? Get a Licensed Driver Waiting at the Airport Door\n\nIf you’re pressed for time and want a licensed local guide to send you a private car at a fair, pre-agreed price — no negotiation, no surprises — message on WhatsApp and your driver will be ready at the arrivals exit.\n\n[WhatsApp +212 671 437 971](https://wa.me/212671437971)\n\n## Private Transfer — Fast & Hassle-Free [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/#mrc-toc)\nFor travelers who’d rather skip the taxi negotiation entirely, a pre-arranged private transfer is the cleanest option. You agree on the price before you fly, a licensed driver picks you up at the arrivals exit holding your name, and the fare doesn’t change regardless of traffic or time of day.\n\nPrivate transfers from Marrakesh airport to the city center typically range from **150 to 250 MAD** depending on the vehicle type and how far into (or near) the medina your accommodation is. It’s more expensive than the bus or a metered taxi, but the time saved and stress avoided is often worth it — especially for families with young children, travelers with significant luggage, or late-night arrivals when taxis are fewer and drivers are more aggressive with pricing.\n\n1\n\nBook in Advance\n\nContact a licensed local operator before your flight. Agree on the fare, vehicle type, and meeting point.\n\n2\n\nLook for Your Name Sign\n\nYour driver will wait in the arrivals hall or directly outside the exit with a sign.\n\n3\n\nPay the Agreed Fare\n\nNo meter, no haggling. You already know the price.\n\n## Ride-Hailing Apps — Careem & inDrive [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/#mrc-toc)\nRide-hailing apps have become increasingly reliable in Marrakesh. Both **Careem** and **inDrive** operate in the city and can be used at the airport. These apps show you the price before you confirm — which removes the biggest source of friction with traditional taxis.\n\nThe typical app-based fare from the airport to the city center runs between **70 and 100 MAD**. You’ll need a working data connection when you land, so it’s worth picking up a local SIM card or making sure your roaming plan is active. The driver comes to you — you share your location via the app — and payment can often be done in-app or in cash.\n\n💡 Practical Tip\n\nDownload and register on Careem or inDrive _before_ you arrive in Morocco. Registration sometimes requires an SMS verification, which can be tricky without a local number.\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/#mrc-toc)\nHow far is Marrakesh airport from the city center? ▼\n\nMarrakesh Menara Airport is approximately 6 km from Jemaa el-Fna square. By taxi or private car it’s a 10–20 minute drive depending on traffic.\n\nWhat is the Marrakech airport taxi price to the medina? ▼\n\nA metered petit taxi should cost between 50 and 80 MAD during the day. At night a 50% surcharge applies, bringing it to around 75–100 MAD. Always insist on the meter. Some drivers may try to quote flat rates of 150–200 MAD — this is significantly above the fair Marrakech airport taxi price and should be declined. For more guidance on dealing with taxi drivers in the city, read our full guide on taxi tips in Marrakech.\n\nHow much does the Marrakech airport to city centre bus cost in 2026? ▼\n\nBus Line 19, the direct Marrakech airport to city centre bus, costs 30 MAD as of May 2026. Some articles incorrectly state the price as 50 MAD — this is not accurate. The correct official fare is 30 MAD, as displayed at the airport bus stop. The ticket is valid for 15 days and includes a free return journey. The bus takes approximately 35–50 minutes to reach Jemaa el-Fna.\n\nCan you walk from Marrakech airport to city centre? ▼\n\nTechnically yes, but it is not recommended. The city centre is about 6 km away — roughly a 60–75 minute walk — along roads that have no dedicated pedestrian paths, no shade, and heavy traffic. While some travelers walk the first 15–20 minutes to reach cheaper city bus stops (5–6 MAD lines), the effort rarely justifies the saving compared to taking Bus Line 19 at 30 MAD directly from the terminal.\n\nIs there a public bus from Marrakesh airport to the city? ▼\n\nYes. Bus Line 19 runs from the airport to Jemaa el-Fna for 30 MAD (May 2026 price). The fare is posted clearly at the stop and on the bus. The journey takes 35–50 minutes. There are also cheaper city buses (5–6 MAD) but these do not stop at the airport — you would need to walk out of the airport grounds first to reach their stops.\n\nCan I use Uber in Marrakesh? ▼\n\nUber is not available in Marrakesh. The main ride-hailing apps operating in the city are Careem and inDrive. Both allow you to see the price before booking.\n\nWhat is the best option for a late-night arrival at Marrakesh airport? ▼\n\nFor late arrivals, a pre-booked private transfer is the safest and most comfortable choice. Fewer taxis are available at night, buses stop running, and drivers are more likely to attempt inflated pricing after dark. Arranging a driver in advance eliminates all of this.\n\nNeed a Private Car? Get a Fair Price Instantly\n\nA licensed local guide can arrange a private vehicle to meet you right at the airport exit — transparent pricing, no surprises. Message on WhatsApp: **+212 671 437 971**\n\n[Message Now](https://wa.me/212671437971)\n\nShare\n\n[Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/sharer.php?u=https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/) [X](https://x.com/intent/post?text=From%C2%A0Marrakesh%20Airport%C2%A0to%20City%20Center%20Every%20Option%20Explained%202026&url=https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/) [Pinterest](https://pinterest.com/pin/create/button/?url=https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/&description=From%C2%A0Marrakesh%20Airport%C2%A0to%20City%20Center%20Every%20Option%20Explained%202026&media=https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/From-Marrakesh-Airport-to-City-Center.webp)",

As of May 2026, the correct and official price is **30 MAD**, as clearly displayed on the fare board at the airport bus stop and confirmed directly on the bus. This ticket also entitles you to a free return journey, valid for 15 days. If a conductor or driver asks for more than 30 MAD, that is not the official rate — the posted fare is what you should pay.\n\n🚌 Bus Line 19 — Key Details (May 2026)\n\nFare: **30 MAD** (includes a free return, valid 15 days) · Route: Airport → Avenue Mohammed VI → Jemaa el-Fna · Frequency: Every 20–30 minutes · Travel time: ~35–50 minutes\n\n![Public bus in Marrakech city](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Bus-Marrakech.webp)\n\nA Marrakech public bus — Bus Line 19 operates the same fleet on the airport route.\n\nLine 19Marrakech Airport Bus 19 Route Map\n\n⏱ ~35–50 min✈RAKMenara AirportDeparture point30 MADAv. Mohammed VIIntermediate stopGuélizNew town centre🏛ENDJemaa el-FnaMedina entranceEvery 20–30 minutesSW ←→ NE\n\nSimplified route diagram for Bus Line 19 — not to scale. Stops shown are principal points along the route.\n\n![Marrakech Bus Line 19 at the airport stop](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Bus-Marrakech-Line-19.jpeg)\n\nBus Line 19 at Marrakesh Menara Airport — fares are clearly displayed on the board.\n\nThe bus is a solid choice if you’re traveling light and don’t mind a journey of about 40 minutes with a few stops. It runs throughout the day but becomes less frequent in the late evening, so if you have a night arrival, verify the schedule in advance. The bus drops you near Jemaa el-Fna, making it convenient for anyone staying in the medina.\n\n🚌 Other City Bus Lines — 5 to 6 MAD\n\nYou may come across mentions of local Marrakesh bus lines that charge just **5 to 6 MAD** per journey. These exist, but there’s an important catch: **none of them serve the airport directly**. To use these cheaper city buses, you would first need to walk out of the airport grounds — a stretch of around 15–20 minutes on foot along a road with no pedestrian infrastructure — before reaching a stop on the main road where these lines run. Unless you are very comfortable with the city’s bus network and traveling with minimal luggage, **Bus Line 19 at 30 MAD remains the practical option** for airport arrivals.\n\nPay in **dirhams** directly to the driver or conductor. Having exact change is appreciated, though not always required.\n\nIn a Hurry? Get a Licensed Driver Waiting at the Airport Door\n\nIf you’re pressed for time and want a licensed local guide to send you a private car at a fair, pre-agreed price — no negotiation, no surprises — message on WhatsApp and your driver will be ready at the arrivals exit.\n\n[WhatsApp +212 671 437 971](https://wa.me/212671437971)\n\n## Private Transfer — Fast & Hassle-Free [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/\\#mrc-toc)\n\nFor travelers who’d rather skip the taxi negotiation entirely, a pre-arranged private transfer is the cleanest option. You agree on the price before you fly, a licensed driver picks you up at the arrivals exit holding your name, and the fare doesn’t change regardless of traffic or time of day.\n\nPrivate transfers from Marrakesh airport to the city center typically range from **150 to 250 MAD** depending on the vehicle type and how far into (or near) the medina your accommodation is. It’s more expensive than the bus or a metered taxi, but the time saved and stress avoided is often worth it — especially for families with young children, travelers with significant luggage, or late-night arrivals when taxis are fewer and drivers are more aggressive with pricing.\n\n1\n\nBook in Advance\n\nContact a licensed local operator before your flight. Agree on the fare, vehicle type, and meeting point.\n\n2\n\nLook for Your Name Sign\n\nYour driver will wait in the arrivals hall or directly outside the exit with a sign.\n\n3\n\nPay the Agreed Fare\n\nNo meter, no haggling. You already know the price.\n\n## Ride-Hailing Apps — Careem & inDrive [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/\\#mrc-toc)\n\nRide-hailing apps have become increasingly reliable in Marrakesh. Both **Careem** and **inDrive** operate in the city and can be used at the airport. These apps show you the price before you confirm — which removes the biggest source of friction with traditional taxis.\n\nThe typical app-based fare from the airport to the city center runs between **70 and 100 MAD**. You’ll need a working data connection when you land, so it’s worth picking up a local SIM card or making sure your roaming plan is active. The driver comes to you — you share your location via the app — and payment can often be done in-app or in cash.\n\n💡 Practical Tip\n\nDownload and register on Careem or inDrive _before_ you arrive in Morocco. Registration sometimes requires an SMS verification, which can be tricky without a local number.\n\n## Can You Walk from Marrakech Airport to City Centre? [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/\\#mrc-toc)\n\nIt’s a question many budget-conscious travelers ask: **can you walk from Marrakech airport to the city centre?** Technically yes — but it is not recommended and here’s why.\n\nThe airport is roughly **6 km from Jemaa el-Fna**. Walking the full distance would take approximately **60 to 75 minutes** under ideal conditions. In reality, the roads leading away from the airport are not designed for pedestrians: there are no dedicated sidewalks for most of the route, the heat can be extreme (especially between May and September), and you’d be navigating with luggage along a busy road with fast-moving traffic.\n\n⚠️ Walking Is Not Practical\n\nWhile walking out of the airport grounds to reach a cheaper city bus stop (5–6 MAD lines) is technically possible, the 15–20 minute walk on an exposed road with no shade or pavement is unpleasant and potentially unsafe, especially at night or with heavy bags. For the small saving versus Bus Line 19, it is rarely worth it.\n\nThe one scenario where a short walk makes sense: if you’re staying near the airport area itself (a handful of hotels are within 1–2 km), walking with light luggage is fine. But for reaching the medina or Gueliz, take the bus, a taxi, or arrange a transfer — your feet will thank you.\n\n## Tips Before You Leave the Airport [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/\\#mrc-toc)\n\nA few small habits can make a big difference when navigating the **Marrakesh airport to center** journey:\n\n✓\n\nHave Dirhams Ready\n\nThere are ATMs in the arrivals hall. Withdraw cash before heading out — [Moroccan dirhams](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/what-currency-does-morocco-use/) are the only accepted payment for local transport.\n\n✓\n\nKnow the Real Fare\n\nPetit taxi from airport to Jemaa el-Fna: ~50–70 MAD (day). Confirm this before entering the taxi. Some drivers will quote 150–200 MAD. That’s too much. Our complete guide on [taxi tips in Marrakech](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/taxi-tips-in-marrakech/) covers every scenario in detail.\n\n✓\n\nStay Calm & Walk Away if Needed\n\nIf a driver is being pushy or won’t agree to the meter, simply move on. There are always other options. Confidence deflects most opportunistic pricing.\n\n✓\n\nRead Up on Scams\n\nAirport taxis are just one setting. For a broader picture of what to watch out for across Marrakesh, the full guide on [tourist scams in Morocco](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/list-of-common-tourist-scams-in-morocco/) is well worth a read before your trip.\n\n✓\n\nCheck Safety Conditions\n\nFor general travel safety, [is Marrakech safe for tourists](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/is-marrakech-safe/)? Overall yes — but preparation makes it even more so.\n\n![Arrivals hall inside Marrakesh Menara Airport](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Marrakesh-Menara-Airport-arrivals-hall.webp)\n\nMarrakesh Menara Airport arrivals hall — ATMs and transport options are all within reach.\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions [↑ TOC](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/\\#mrc-toc)\n\nHow far is Marrakesh airport from the city center?▼\n\nMarrakesh Menara Airport is approximately 6 km from Jemaa el-Fna square. By taxi or private car it’s a 10–20 minute drive depending on traffic.\n\nWhat is the Marrakech airport taxi price to the medina?▼\n\nA metered petit taxi should cost between 50 and 80 MAD during the day. At night a 50% surcharge applies, bringing it to around 75–100 MAD. Always insist on the meter. Some drivers may try to quote flat rates of 150–200 MAD — this is significantly above the fair Marrakech airport taxi price and should be declined. For more guidance on dealing with taxi drivers in the city, read our full guide on taxi tips in Marrakech.\n\nHow much does the Marrakech airport to city centre bus cost in 2026?▼\n\nBus Line 19, the direct Marrakech airport to city centre bus, costs 30 MAD as of May 2026. Some articles incorrectly state the price as 50 MAD — this is not accurate. The correct official fare is 30 MAD, as displayed at the airport bus stop. The ticket is valid for 15 days and includes a free return journey. The bus takes approximately 35–50 minutes to reach Jemaa el-Fna.\n\nCan you walk from Marrakech airport to city centre?▼\n\nTechnically yes, but it is not recommended. The city centre is about 6 km away — roughly a 60–75 minute walk — along roads that have no dedicated pedestrian paths, no shade, and heavy traffic. While some travelers walk the first 15–20 minutes to reach cheaper city bus stops (5–6 MAD lines), the effort rarely justifies the saving compared to taking Bus Line 19 at 30 MAD directly from the terminal.\n\nIs there a public bus from Marrakesh airport to the city?▼\n\nYes. Bus Line 19 runs from the airport to Jemaa el-Fna for 30 MAD (May 2026 price). The fare is posted clearly at the stop and on the bus. The journey takes 35–50 minutes. There are also cheaper city buses (5–6 MAD) but these do not stop at the airport — you would need to walk out of the airport grounds first to reach their stops.\n\nCan I use Uber in Marrakesh?▼\n\nUber is not available in Marrakesh. The main ride-hailing apps operating in the city are Careem and inDrive. Both allow you to see the price before booking.\n\nWhat is the best option for a late-night arrival at Marrakesh airport?▼\n\nFor late arrivals, a pre-booked private transfer is the safest and most comfortable choice. Fewer taxis are available at night, buses stop running, and drivers are more likely to attempt inflated pricing after dark. Arranging a driver in advance eliminates all of this.\n\nNeed a Private Car? Get a Fair Price Instantly\n\nA licensed local guide can arrange a private vehicle to meet you right at the airport exit — transparent pricing, no surprises. Message on WhatsApp: **+212 671 437 971**\n\n[Message Now](https://wa.me/212671437971)\n\nShare\n\n[Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/sharer.php?u=https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/ \"Facebook\") [X](https://x.com/intent/post?text=From%C2%A0Marrakesh%20Airport%C2%A0to%20City%20Center%20Every%20Option%20Explained%202026&url=https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/ \"X\") [Pinterest](https://pinterest.com/pin/create/button/?url=https://moroccantraveltrips.com/marrakesh-airport-to-center/&description=From%C2%A0Marrakesh%20Airport%C2%A0to%20City%20Center%20Every%20Option%20Explained%202026&media=https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/From-Marrakesh-Airport-to-City-Center.webp \"Pinterest\")\n\n### Others\n\n[![](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/things-to-do-in-morocco-390x220.webp)](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/things-to-do-in-morocco/)\n\n### [Things to do in Morocco by region: what fits in one week](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/things-to-do-in-morocco/)\n\n[![](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/tangier-featured-en-390x220.webp)](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/visit-tangier-morocco/)\n\n### [Tangier, Morocco Travel Guide: Medina, Kasbah & Day Trips](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/visit-tangier-morocco/)\n\n[![](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/activities-in-marrakech-390x220.webp)](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/activities-in-marrakech/)\n\n### [Activities in Marrakech: Camel, Quad, Balloon & Medina (2026)](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/activities-in-marrakech/)\n\n[![A blue house featuring vibrant yellow and green accents on its exterior. Majorelle Garden & Yves Saint Laurent Museum](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/Le_jardin_des_majorelle_16-390x220.webp)](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/jardin-majorelle-marrakech-guide/)\n\n### [Jardin Majorelle Marrakech: Tickets, hours & visiting order](https://moroccantraveltrips.com/jardin-majorelle-marrakech-guide/)\n\nreCAPTCHA\n\nRecaptcha requires verification.\n\nprotected by **reCAPTCHA**",

Plan your perfect Casablanca trip today.\n\nCulture & artIconic spotsCool neighbourhoodsLocal dishesWhere to eat\n\n- Must visit\n\n### Villa des Arts de Casablanca\n\nLeading museum for modern and contemporary Moroccan art in a restored Art Deco villa. A strong introduction to national artists and Casablanca culture.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Villa+des+Arts+de+Casablanca+Casablanca) [Visit website](https://www.fondationona.ma/)\n\n- Must visit\n\n### Museum of Moroccan Judaism\n\nMuseum dedicated to Moroccan Judaism, memory, and cultural heritage. It is unique in the Arab world and offers thoughtful, well-curated displays.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Museum+of+Moroccan+Judaism+Casablanca) [Visit website](http://www.fondation-patrimoine-judaisme-marocain.org/)\n\n- Recommended\n\n### Musée Abderrahman Slaoui\n\nArt Deco villa museum focused on Casablanca’s urban history, architecture, and identity. Useful for understanding how the modern city developed.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Musee+Abderrahman+Slaoui+Casablanca) [Visit website](https://musee-as.ma/)\n\n- Must visit\n\n### Hassan II Mosque\n\nMorocco’s largest mosque stands over the Atlantic with a vast prayer hall and a landmark minaret. A defining symbol of Casablanca and a top architectural visit.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Hassan+II+Mosque+Casablanca) [Visit website](https://fmh2.ma/)\n\n- Must visit\n\n### Place Mohammed V\n\nMain square framed by major civic buildings and palms. It reflects Casablanca’s colonial urban planning and remains a central place for city walks and photos.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Place+Mohammed+V+Casablanca) [Visit website](https://casablancaheritage.ma/)\n\n- Must visit\n\n### Old Medina\n\nHistoric district with old walls, lanes, and traditional life. It offers a glimpse of pre-colonial Casablanca and is best explored on foot by day.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Old+Medina+Casablanca) [Visit website](https://casablancaheritage.ma/)\n\n- Must visit\n\n### Corniche Ain Diab\n\nSeafront corniche with ocean views, promenades, and public spaces. It is ideal for walking, people-watching, and seeing modern Casablanca by the Atlantic.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Corniche+Ain+Diab+Casablanca) [Visit website](https://www.casablanca.ma/)\n\n- Must visit\n\n### Boulevard Mohammed V\n\nHistoric central boulevard lined with palms and major architecture. It is one of the best urban walks for experiencing Casablanca’s civic core.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Boulevard+Mohammed+V+Casablanca) [Visit website](https://casablancaheritage.ma/)\n\n- Recommended\n\n### Casablanca Marina\n\nUpscale waterfront district centered on the mosque and marina. It combines broad walkways, sea views, and polished public space near major landmarks.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Casablanca+Marina+Casablanca) [Visit website](https://www.marinacasablanca.com/)\n\n- Gluten-free\n\n### Tagine With Prunes\n\nSlow-cooked beef or lamb stew with prunes, almonds, and warm spices. A festive Moroccan classic often served for family meals and special occasions.\n\n### Couscous\n\nSteamed semolina couscous topped with vegetables and often lamb, chicken, or beef. Traditionally eaten on Fridays and central to Moroccan home cooking.\n\n- Gluten-free\n\n### Harira\n\nHearty soup of tomatoes, lentils, chickpeas, and spices, sometimes with meat. A beloved comfort dish, especially popular for breaking the Ramadan fast.\n\n### Le Cabestan\n\nElegant oceanfront dining in La Corniche, known for refined Moroccan and seafood dishes in a polished fine-dining setting.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Le+Cabestan+Casablanca)\n\n### La Sqala\n\nStylish restaurant in the Habous area serving classic Moroccan dishes and pastilla in a sophisticated riad-inspired atmosphere.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=La+Sqala+Casablanca)\n\n### Restaurant Al Mounia\n\nBeloved traditional stop near the old medina for tagines, couscous, and grilled meats in a simple, authentic local setting.\n\n[View on map](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Restaurant+Al+Mounia+Casablanca)\n\nBudgetGetting around\n\n### Moroccan dirham (MAD, د.م.)\n\nModerate for North Africa: hotels and dining are fair, taxis and trams are cheap, but upscale seaside venues cost more.\n\n#### Average meal costs\n\nBudget40-80 MAD for street food or a simple meal.\n\nMid-range120-220 MAD per person for a restaurant meal.\n\nFine dining300+ MAD per person at upscale restaurants.\n\nCoffee15-30 MAD for coffee or cappuccino.\n\n#### Tipping culture\n\nService may be included, but 5-10% is common in restaurants. Round up in cafes. For taxis, round up or add 5-10 MAD for helpful service.\n\n### Casatramway\n\n[Get the app](https://casatramway.ma/)\n\nOfficial public transport app\n\n#### Prices\n\nSingle ticket8 MAD\n\nDay pass/,\n\n#### To the city center\n\nAvenue des FAR (CTM) -> Place Mohammed V, Casablanca\n\nDistance3 km\n\nTravel time15 min\n\n#### Best option: Tramway T1\n\nPrice12 MAD\n\nDuration15 min\n\n[Get directions](https://www.google.com/maps/dir/?api=1&origin=Avenue+des+FAR+(CTM),+Casablanca&destination=Place+Mohammed+V,+Casablanca) [Get tickets](https://casatramway.ma/)\n\n- Buy tram tickets from station machines before boarding; validators and inspectors are used regularly.\n- Contactless bank card payment is not generally available onboard Casablanca trams or buses; carry cash or use station ticket machines where available.\n\nPlan your trip to Casablanca\n\n### Plan your visit to Casablanca\n\nGet destination-focused answers for the end of your trip, including what to do, when to go, practical entry details, and tips for making the most of your time in Casablanca.\n\n### What currency is used in Casablanca and is it better to use cash or card?\n\nThe currency used in Morocco is the Moroccan Dirham (MAD). Travelers often find it convenient to use cash for smaller purchases and markets, while cards are widely accepted in hotels, restaurants, and larger stores, making a combination of both payment methods ideal.\n\n### What are the main safety concerns and scams to watch out for in Casablanca?\n\nIn Casablanca, travelers should be cautious of pickpocketing and petty theft, especially in crowded areas like markets and public transport. Common scams include overcharging by taxis that do not use meters, and unofficial guides offering unsolicited services. It is advisable to agree on taxi fares beforehand and stay vigilant in busy tourist spots. Avoid flashing valuables and be wary of distractions used by scammers to divert attention.\n\n### What are some general packing tips for comfort and security?\n\nPack lightweight, versatile clothing suitable for the destination's climate, and use secure, lockable luggage to protect belongings. Keep essential items like travel documents, medication, and valuables in a carry-on bag for easy access. Use packing organizers or cubes to maximize space and maintain order.
